# CLAUDE.md — Archive

**Created July 31, 2026.** CLAUDE.md's own doc-discipline rule (written July 28) said an archive file "should exist once this file's Current state section is next pruned in full." That prune happened July 31 during a full oversight-doc audit, so this file now exists.

**What this is:** the dated "Current state (July 24)" and "Prior state (July 18 / 20 / 22 / 23)" sections, moved here verbatim. Everything below was true when written and much of it has since been superseded. **Do not treat anything in this file as current** — CLAUDE.md's own Current State section, the Security note, and the Known landmines section are the live truth, and `patch_notes.html` holds the full incident narrative for any of it.

**Why keep it at all:** these sections record real reasoning, real incidents, and real decisions with their original justifications. Deleting them would lose the "why" behind rules that are still enforced. Reading them is for archaeology, not for orientation.

---

## Current state (July 24, 2026, session end) — don't re-discover this
- **API-auth fix: confirmed genuinely live after a real incident earlier the same day.** A bare "yes" was wrongly treated as confirmation the Vercel env vars were set; merged, then reverted same-session once Alex clarified they weren't. Re-applied once he set both AND gave specific confirmation, then independently verified against production (`/api/oracle` returns a real `401` unauthenticated — proof `RPGACE_API_SECRET` has a genuine server-side value). Standing lesson written into CLAUDE.md's resolved-incident note: a bare "yes" is not sufficient evidence for a Tier-3 merge with this blast radius.
- **RLS parity fix — done.** `bookworm_books`/`bookworm_chapters`/`bibliography` now have RLS enabled with the same permissive `anon_all` policy every other table already uses, closing the "not even nominally covered" gap. Confirmed via `get_advisors`.
- **Approach B phase 1 — built, deployed, and gating-verified.** A real restriction on top of the parity fix: `api/data-write.js` + `RPGACE.sb.secureWrite()` route writes through a server-side endpoint using a new `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, bypassing RLS entirely. `node --check` clean, merged and deployed, `401` confirmed on an unauthenticated request. **✅ RESOLVED July 24, later same session — a real authenticated write succeeding end-to-end, independently confirmed, not assumed.** Alex used the new Review Queue dashboard card for real; a direct Supabase query (not a doc claim) found 8 genuine writes between 23:09-23:16 UTC — 90 minutes after the RLS flip on these exact tables — 3 `taxonomy_proposals` updates, 5 `taxonomy_links` confirms, plus one full accept chain that also INSERTed a new `taxonomy_tree` leaf and a `taxonomy_decision_log` audit row with a matching `node_id`. Since `anon` is SELECT-only on all of these post-flip and the client never authenticates as anything but `anon`, these writes could only have gone through `/api/data-write`'s service-role path. This is real proof for UPDATE **and** INSERT through the proxy, on 4 tables — DELETE through the proxy, the 6 deliberately-excluded tables, and the encyclopedia's `onConflict` upsert path remain genuinely unconfirmed, named honestly rather than folded into a blanket "it works" claim. (Real discriminator worth keeping as a technique: Postgres-side bulk writes carry 6-decimal `now()` timestamps; this app's own writes carry 3-decimal `new Date().toISOString()` timestamps — this is what separated 8 real clicks from 30 unrelated rows in the same table that shared one bulk-update microsecond, see the taxonomy backlog note below.)
- **Approach B phase 2, real gap found and fixed.** Alex asked to expand the write-proxy to the remaining ~17 tables, 3 at a time. Real evidence-gathering before each batch found the codebase actually has **three distinct raw-fetch idioms** for writing to Supabase (`RPGACE.sb.insert/update/del`, `fetch(RPGACE.sb.url(table))`, and inline `RPGACE.CONFIG.supabase.url + '/rest/v1/...'`) — phase 1's own verification only ever searched the first two, so **`taxonomy_tree`, `taxonomy_nodes`, `bookworm_books`, and `bookworm_chapters` were never actually fully migrated** despite being logged as complete. Found and fixed the same session it was discovered, via an exhaustive re-check across all three idioms for every table touched — now genuinely zero remaining direct-anon writes, confirmed by grep, not assumed. Also caught `intel_watchlist` reachable through an already-migrated wrapper (`intelDelete._sbDel`) but missing from the allowlist — a real regression that would have 403'd on the next delete, fixed before it could bite. 15 tables now covered total (7 from phase 1 + `intel_reports`/`intel_bibliography`/`intel_watchlist`/`encyclopedia`/`reference_tracks`/`conid_pot`/`content_productions`/`video_jobs` from phase 2 batches 1-2), merged and deployed. Remaining out of scope, split honestly by real evidence: `chronicles_finance` (1 real call site), `oracle_dev_suggestions` (1, via a dynamic-table-name fetch), `taxonomy_decision_log` (1, fire-and-forget audit log) are real candidates for a future batch 3. ~~`journal`, `rpgace_agendas`, `intel_jobs`, `encyclopedia_insights` have **zero real client write call sites at all**~~ — **corrected July 24 (real /Godmode audit, /Routine interconnection pass): this was false.** The exhaustive grep that produced this claim only ever searched `rpgace_core.js` — it never checked `main.js`, which makes **27 raw REST calls with the anon key**, including 8 real writes across exactly these 4 tables (`encyclopedia` POST/PATCH/DELETE, `journal` POST/DELETE, `encyclopedia_insights` POST/DELETE, `rpgace_agendas` POST, `intel_jobs` POST). Not currently a live break (RLS on these tables is still `anon_all`, unflipped), but it means Approach B's real coverage claim needs a FIFTH known write idiom added to the standing verification list: `main.js`'s own hardcoded-credential raw fetches, a separate file entirely from every idiom checked so far. `rpgace_shifts` is written by an external Python browser-use script outside the app entirely (already known); `system_updates` is written only by Claude Code sessions directly via Supabase MCP, never from client JS. Standing lesson, now sharper: "zero remaining writes" claims need to search every known write pattern **in every file**, not just the most obvious pattern in the most obvious file — a single grep pattern, or a single file's worth of grepping, is not exhaustive verification.
- **Taxonomy backlog triage — done, with a real process correction along the way.** Real Supabase evidence (not the prior audit's "107, largely garbage" framing) showed the 60 pending `taxonomy_proposals` were two genuinely different batches: 30 unscored pre-engine rows (rejected outright) and 30 real-scored July 19 rows plus 47 fusion links (genuinely good on inspection, left pending for Alex's own full review). A real process gap surfaced and closed: this decision didn't get its own dedicated verbatim backlog file the way the project's convention requires — `item7_process_debate_2026-07-24.txt` closes that, plus two new CLAUDE.md rule clarifications (rule 4 now names review-queue backlogs explicitly, rule 5 now covers backlog-clearing decisions, not just build decisions).

Replaces the old ad hoc "update oversight"/"end session" convention with a named, repeatable one — see the updated Oversight logging section below.
- **Claude Code fallback lane — built for real, not just diagrammed.** Following the diagram below, Alex confirmed the Tier-3 table creation and ran a real `/debate` to decide scope ("what does auto-resume actually mean") before any code was written — full record in `claude_fallback_build_plan_2026-07-24.txt`. Shipped: `oracle_fallback_queue` (real table + `context`/`resumed_at` columns), `bookworm_chapters.fallback_pending`, the shared detection/enqueue/sweep choke point, `bookworm._resumeFromFallback`, `taxonomyTree._resumeSilentProposeFromFallback`, and a new hourly "RPGACE Fallback Drain" Claude Code Remote Routine mirroring the Morning Brief's exact proven pattern. `node --check` clean. Not yet hand-tested against a genuine credit-exhaustion event — forcing one on purpose would mean actually draining Alex's real API credits.

OpenMontage gets no diagram entry anywhere (its verdict was adopt-as-a-separate-tool, never RPGACE-embedded, so it has no attach point on a map of RPGACE's own internal flows). `oracle_fallback_queue` still does not exist — creating it remains a real Tier-3 migration still needing Alex's own explicit go-ahead, separate from his general "sounds good" on the idea.

Oracle command. **Fable failed on usage credits dispatching Phase 1-2 — the SECOND time this exact failure has happened this project** (first was July 20) — reported honestly, Alex authorized substituting Sonnet, now a named recurring pattern worth budgeting for. (2) A real standing rule: "reduplication is always a priority... same processes must go through one pipeline or function if steps are identical" — promoted to CLAUDE.md rule 8. **His own wording had a genuine word mixup** (he meant "deduplication," not "reduplication" — an opposite-meaning real word) — caught and corrected the SAME session when he flagged it, verbatim quotes preserved, only the surrounding prose fixed, and the rule extended to explicitly cover taxonomy's own existing dedup precedent (`_continueAnalyzingInBackground`'s per-chapter leaf-name dedup) alongside `intelDedup`. Fixed; `_context.js` is now the single source of truth. Cannot deploy until Alex sets two Vercel env vars (`CORRECT_PW`, `RPGACE_API_SECRET`) himself — confirmed Claude Code has no tool access to do this directly (checked every available Vercel MCP tool). ### Prior state (July 22, 2026, session end) — still true unless superseded above
- **Council of 5 shipped as a real Oracle command (15th Prod. Oracle command)**, directly answering whether RPGACE's runtime API could "get" the Claude Code Skills built July 20 — it can't (two genuinely separate mechanisms, checked against the claude-api skill's real docs, not assumed) — so the real answer was porting the protocol into the app's own existing command pattern instead. Reuses 100% existing infrastructure (`prodOraclePanel.CMDS` + `fillGaps`/`sendToOracle`), no main.js edit.
- **Aintergration applied to a ~150-chapter system-design book, all 3 batches processed.** Real, load-bearing scope discipline established and held across all three passes: most chapters are generic CS fundamentals with no adoption decision attached, and forcing a verdict on each would be empty ceremony — triaged for real signal instead of grinding through every chapter uniformly. Full findings logged as Aintergration Worked Precedents 4-6 in this file's Invokable Frameworks section. Two real, previously-undiscovered security findings surfaced this way: **every `/api/*.js` endpoint has zero server-side auth** (bigger exposure than the known RLS gap — billing/account-action risk, not just data risk), and **`CORRECT_PW` is readable via view-source regardless of where it's stored**, since the check runs entirely in client JS — both are one underlying architecture decision, not two separate quick fixes, deliberately held back for a dedicated sequenced pass rather than rushed. Also confirmed directly by Alex: **no Supabase backup/PITR exists at all** (was an open question, now settled fact) — Tier 3, his call on when/whether to fix.
- **Oracle gained real self-awareness + a two-way Claude Code bridge, 6 pieces, all shipped, none hand-tested yet:** `oracleAppGrounding` (reads `dashDeck`'s live cards + an honest status digest, keyword-gated, costs nothing when it doesn't trigger), `oracleFetchGuard` (hardens Oracle against prompt injection from fetched web/YouTube/Instagram content — a direct answer to a risk this session itself had flagged earlier), `oracleDevBridge` (a "🧪 Flag for Claude Code" button on any substantial Oracle reply, writing to a new `oracle_dev_suggestions` table), `taxonomy_decision_log` (an append-only audit trail written at `_insertNewSteps`'s single real choke point — the first event-sourcing-style pattern in the app, turns "which code path produced this" from a forensic Supabase query into a plain read), Council of 5's own conversation-capture button (`fillGaps`'s new opt-in `opts.allowConversationCapture` — captures the real chat instead of Alex re-typing a summary, backward compatible with the other 40+ command entries that already use `fillGaps`), and a real Claude Code Remote Routine (`trig_015uRjGVrdmiwc5zxq47mMA6`, daily 6am UTC) delivering an actually-pushed Morning Brief instead of pull-based — caught and fixed a real problem before it could fail silently (the trigger has no MCP connector access, so it was rewritten to curl Supabase's REST API directly, exactly like `main.js`'s own client code already does).
- **Session-start check added**: any session working on RPGACE now checks `oracle_dev_suggestions` for `status='new'` rows before other work — still pull, not push (a true push needs a Supabase webhook wired to a Claude Code Remote trigger, flagged as a future idea only).
- **All six oversight docs updated in the same session** — CLAUDE.md, patch_notes.html, manual.html, system_flow_map.md, interconnection_map.md, and minotaur_map.html (a genuinely new "River VIII," since this is the first wing in the whole map where water leaves RPGACE entirely and reaches a different world — a future Claude Code session — rather than ending at Alex's own screen).
- **Nothing from today is hand-tested.** All of it is `node --check`-clean / div-balance-clean / independently re-verified against real source before being logged, live on `main`, awaiting Alex's click-through.
- **QoL cleanup (2 passes) + a real nav-lag incident, root-caused twice.** Pass 1: sidebar load, schedule XP-per-view bug, Research Lab tab-wrapper removal, tighter list collapsing. Pass 2 (real answers, not guessed defaults): shift-sync cache fix (an external Python browser-use script writes `rpgace_shifts` outside the app — added to the no-cache table list), Agents folded into Oracle AI (moved live DOM, not rebuilt), Encyclopedia/Journal get real search + the new audit-trail tables surfaced as read-only entries. **Incident, same day:** the sidebar fix briefly made `leftNav._buildDrawer()` run outside the `rpgace:ready` gate, aborting ~25 of ~40 modules' registration mid-parse — caught live from a pasted console log, reverted immediately. The underlying bug re-surfaced contained (not catastrophic) and was root-caused for real: `leftNav` registers before `config` in file order, so `leftNav.init()` always ran before `RPGACE.CONFIG` existed — fixed with a defensive guard + retry, not just the hotfix revert. **A second, deeper, systemic root cause found afterward**, from the separate complaint "nav doesn't respond for ~10s after login": `onReady()` gated the entire module-registration cascade behind the `window.load` event (waiting on every image/font), when the real dependency — login — is completely decoupled from page-load timing. Switched to `DOMContentLoaded`-equivalent + a 50ms delay, a systemic fix improving every module's startup, not just leftNav's. Cache-busting version string bumped multiple times same day after finding it hadn't moved in days despite dozens of edits — standing lesson: bump it every time `rpgace_core.js` changes.
- **RPGACE is now a real installable PWA** (Android/desktop "Add to Home Screen"/"Install app") — `manifest.json` + `sw.js` (deliberately network-first, not cache-first, to avoid making the stale-cache bug class above permanent) + a generated gold-sword icon set in `icons/`, registered via a new `pwaInstall` module (no new `<script>` tag). `vercel.json`'s existing SPA catch-all rewrite would have silently served `index.html` for the manifest/SW/icon requests — fixed with explicit passthrough rewrites before shipping, not after. Also added: a full-screen boot-loader overlay, visible from first paint, hidden only once `rpgace:ready` confirms every module has registered — the login gate itself never appears mid-race now. Pure-CSS 6-second fallback, zero JS dependency, so it can never trap a user if script execution stalls.
- **The profile stat card rebuilt onto real, persisted data (GODMODE/Council of 5/Aintergration, Alex's own request).** Real finding: HP/MP bars and "Streak" were 100% cosmetic — confirmed via grep, zero code anywhere ever touched them — and XP/Level lived only in in-memory `STATE`, resetting to zero every page load. Real signal already existed unused in Supabase (journal, encyclopedia_insights, taxonomy_proposals, bookworm_chapters, reference_tracks, content_productions). Rebuilt as a real weighted career score with two deliberately SEPARATE lanes — **Output** (real shipped content only) and **Growth** (learning/knowledge-tree activity) — specifically so taxonomy busywork can never visually substitute for actually shipping something (a real Goodhart's-law risk raised and addressed before building, not after). Considered and explicitly rejected: variable-ratio/randomised reward schedules (the mobile-game "loot box" pattern) — effective at compulsive engagement, wrong shape for a tool whose own governing rule is real output.
- **Recent Wins → renamed "The Chronicles" (Alex's own name), interrogated before building further.** Round 1: every feed row became a real clickable detail popup (What was done / Outcome / Where stored / Why significant), reusing `dashDeck._popup` rather than a new one-off overlay. Round 2 (after real interrogation, verbatim Q&A in `chronicles_spec_backlog.txt`, since this touches real financial record-keeping): a full dedicated `#page-chronicles` log page (search, type filters, full history) plus a new `chronicles_finance` Supabase table for real sale/expense entries — explicitly a **personal-visibility tracker, not bookkeeping-grade** (real receipts still needed at actual tax time). Phylum 17 "Negotium" (already reserved in the phyla config for conceptual business/tax knowledge, never built out) was deliberately left untouched — a transaction ledger and a conceptual knowledge phylum are different data shapes, kept separate on purpose.
- **This oversight sweep itself: a new `/scope` skill, and Chronicles now logs Claude Code's own updates.** `/scope` (`.claude/skills/scope/SKILL.md`) is a reusable evidence-gathering skill — git log + live Supabase state + doc line-counts, organized into digestible grouped categories (bugs/features/infra/security/planned-not-built) — built specifically so an "update oversight" pass doesn't miss something or drown in blow-by-blow detail; it's the GODMODE evidence pass for oversight work specifically, not a separate ceremony on top of it. A new `system_updates` table (RLS + anon_all) + a new Chronicles filter/detail-formatter means every real Claude Code change to RPGACE now shows up in Chronicles alongside Alex's own real activity — one shared timeline, not two separate records. **Standing rule going forward, same discipline as the `patch_notes.html` rule**: any session that ships a real, user-visible change to RPGACE inserts a short row into `system_updates` (title/summary/category) in the same session, so Chronicles stays a live, honest record — not a retrofit project.
- **Real inconsistency caught and fixed mid-build, worth naming as a lesson**: the finance detail popup's "Why significant" text claimed a sale "feeds the Output-lane picture" — the code explicitly does NOT fold finance into that score (confirmed via interrogation, a deliberate separate-lane decision). Caught by re-reading the code against its own stated design before this sweep, not by Alex reporting it — the exact kind of doc-vs-code drift GODMODE evidence-gathering exists to catch, including inside a single session's own just-written work, not only across older docs.
- **The Taxonomy Sorting Agent and the Jargon Encyclopedia/Book Knowledge items it was blocking — real doc-drift found and fixed.** Before building the two "dry riverbed" items Alex asked for, traced the actual call chain (not the docs): `bookworm._decidePlacementScored` is a one-line pass-through to `phylumPath.decidePlacementScored` — the exact same function `encSync`/`ciAutoPropose` already call for non-book inputs. Book and non-book insights have shared ONE placement engine since the July 19 unification; the "Taxonomy Sorting Agent" as this file previously described it (see the struck-through line above) was already effectively built and the docs simply never caught up. What it was actually described as blocking — a queryable book-knowledge source and a read-only Jargon Encyclopedia — genuinely didn't exist. Both shipped as pure Postgres views over data already being written (`book_knowledge` unnests `bookworm_chapters.insights`, `jargon_encyclopedia` selects `taxonomy_tree WHERE node_type='leaf'`) — zero new tables, zero new writes, per Alex's explicit "build in to the existing rather than build on top." A real `security_definer_view` lint was found via `get_advisors` and fixed (`security_invoker=true`) before shipping. Jargon Encyclopedia surfaced as a button inside Phylum Path's existing page, not a new nav destination.
- **General-knowledge audit redesigned around a new `/debate` skill, not built as originally scoped.** Alex confirmed general/generic Claude knowledge should never be injected directly into the tree. New `/debate` skill (`.claude/skills/debate/SKILL.md`, alongside `omnitrix` and `scope`): structured adversarial rounds (both sides attack, then both defend, then Council of 5 cleans up both cases) specifically so comparing Claude's general training against RPGACE's own gathered content never has the side effect of writing to the tree — a debate "won" still requires an explicit separate build decision afterward, never automatic. **Not yet run** — Alex asked to hold off rather than pick a topic in this pass.

### Prior state (July 20, 2026, session end) — still true unless superseded above

Big widgets (Bookworm, Knowledge Gaps, Content Pipeline) live inside card popups as MOVED live DOM nodes (pattern: `_ensureStash`/`_stashWidget(id,force)`); quest board relocated to the Agenda page. Popup buttons that open lower-z-index surfaces must `closeWidgetPopup(id)` first (popups are 99999; drawers/panels are 9998-10110).

One pre-existing (not this session's) unclosed `<div>` found in index.html, confirmed harmless and NOT introduced by this edit, flagged for a future dedicated pass.
- **Real limitation confirmed, worth remembering:** Fable 5 can run out of usage credits mid-task — a background dispatch will fail with a plain task-notification, not silently hang. When it does, do not substitute a different model into the Fable role without Alex's explicit say-so; report the failure and ask. **Confirmed a second time, July 23** (the `/5thDimension` Phase 1+2 evidence-audit dispatch) — this is now a real, recurring pattern on Fable specifically, not a one-off. Both times Alex's answer was to substitute Sonnet rather than wait/run `/usage-credits`. Standing lesson: Fable's fresh-context edge is real for genuinely large audits, but weigh its real failure-rate against just running the task Sonnet-direct from the start, per Omnitrix's own "most tasks don't need a dedicated fresh-context agent" reasoning.

Chapter 1 (`chapter_index` 0) sits `in_progress`, 6 insights, 3 approved pre-queue / 3 still pending, no active heartbeat. **The real open test is narrower than previously stated: one clean chapter through the POST-retune unified engine + July 20 UI** — resume chapter 1 or open the next unstudied one — not "first-ever combined run." Also found, not previously logged: a **duplicate `bookworm_books` row** for this same book (`87268196-…`, all chapters pending, zero real progress) sitting alongside the real one — harmless but worth knowing before picking "the" book to resume.
  2. **Fusion links: 21 confirmed / 47 pending (68 total)**, not "6 confirmed" — badly stale, likely last updated right after the first few links shipped.
  3. **107-item review-queue backlog** nobody had logged: 60 pending `taxonomy_proposals` + 47 pending fusion links, largely generated by the pre-July-19 engines the placement-rules audit itself found produced bad output. `video_jobs` = 0 rows confirmed (Beat Log has never once persisted — F16/F17/F18 have no data to test against, the feature closest to the governing rule has never actually been used).
  Full done/missing/planned/discussed breakdown and the Council-of-5-scrutinized forward plan (highest priority: hand-test July 20's 3 unverified builds, THEN one clean PDF chapter through the new engine) delivered to Alex in chat, July 20 — not duplicated into a new file (Council-of-5 pushback on Fable's own suggestion: this repo already has too many stale point-in-time snapshot docs at root — see Missing/Housekeeping — adding another would repeat the exact problem).

- **Taxonomy: 10 of 21 phyla live in Phylum Path** (`ENABLED_PHYLA: [1..10]`), all through the 7-step Phylum Development Framework (spec → keywords → tree build → data repair → enable → fusion pass → hand-test). Current architecture: `detectChapterListByOracle()` (model reads ONLY the table of contents, self-verifies against 5 explicit checks, recircles on any numbering gap) + `resolveChapterHeadingsMechanically()` (100% deterministic text search, character-fuzzy title matching, strict reading-order-bounded, title-only fallback). ## Archived Sep 15, 2026 — HABITS/Cooking full build narrative (Sep 10–14) and the Galaxy Development Framework's full G1–G109 build narrative (Aug 13–26)

**Why this is here:** a real `/paranoia`+`/free-for-all-debate` pass (Sep 15 2026) found CLAUDE.md's own July 31 doc-discipline rule — "when an entry's underlying event is done, verified, and no longer being actively re-litigated, collapse it to one durable-fact bullet" — had gone unenforced since. These two sections alone (HABITS/Cooking, and the Galaxy Map's own build history under "The 4 layers (c/d/e/f)") accounted for 227KB of CLAUDE.md's then-502KB total. Both are fully resolved as of this date; both are moved here verbatim and replaced in CLAUDE.md with durable one-line facts, per the file's own stated rule. **Do not treat anything below as current** — CLAUDE.md's own Current State section and Known Landmines section are the live truth.

### HABITS domain — full Sep 10–14 2026 build narrative (verbatim)

### HABITS (new domain, Sep 10 2026)
- **Cooking is the first real HABITS module, shipped Sep 10 via a real /CEO Loop 1 spec + Loop 2 build.** Full record: `records/2026-09/habits_cooking_domain_interrogation_spec_2026-09-10.txt` (the interrogation) and `patch_notes.html`'s own Sep 10 card (the build). Built entirely inside the existing app (`cookingOracle` module, `RPGACE.register()`/`dashDeck._popup()`, no new stack/repo/framework — Alex's own direct correction after an initial "new stack" misreading). Real Supabase schema: `recipes`/`ingredients`/`recipe_ingredients`/`ingredient_prices` + 4 views (cost totals, stale-price flags, low-value-buy flags, `ingredient_frequency`/`ingredient_frequency_by_month` — real long-term eating-trend tracking, gated on `journal.status='cooked'`, not merely saved) + a small `habits_config` table (habits toggle, unit system, default shop, real oven(2)/stovetop(4) capacity). Generate via the existing Oracle chat pipeline (a `RECIPE_JSON:` trailer, same convention as `DIRECTOR_CHOSEN:`/`EDL_JSON:`), review (real serving scaler, a step timeline tagged `prep_before_cooking`/`actual_cooking`/`prep_while_cooking`/`baking` with real per-step timers), Save writes `recipes`/`recipe_ingredients` + one `journal` row (`entry_type='recipe'`, `status='saved'`) — never regenerated per view. A saved recipe's Journal entry gets a real "Mark Cooked" button (`refreshJournalDisplay`'s own small conditional addition, main.js legacy block). Verified via 5 real headless-Chromium functional checks (card shape, module method surface, popup fields, `RECIPE_JSON` parsing, settings fields) — all passed. **H6 shipped Sep 13 2026** (this line's own "not yet built" claim about H6 is stale from that date — kept here as historical narrative per this file's own doc-discipline, corrected in place per rule 16): a real `planned_cooks` table (recipe_ids/schedule/scheduled_at/agenda_id, same `anon_read_only`/`authenticated_all` posture) backs a real in-progress multi-recipe cook session in `cookingOracle` — save a recipe, optionally add more (picked from existing saved recipes or freshly generated), then "📅 Schedule cook time" computes a real greedy longest-recipe-first interval-packing schedule (a genuine, verified joint hands(capacity 1)+equipment(oven/stovetop, CONFIG-set) feasibility check — a real bug where equipment capacity was tracked but never enforced was caught and fixed via a standalone Node test before it ever shipped) and shows a read-only proposed timeline; Accept writes `planned_cooks` and pushes one real agenda entry via the existing shared `scheduleToCalendar()` (rule 8). Each component recipe keeps its own `journal` row untouched — H3's `cooked_at`/FK contract and H1's `ingredient_frequency` view are unaffected. Verified via a standalone algorithm test (oven-capacity-1 serializes, capacity-2 parallelizes with a 3rd waiting) plus a real headless-Chromium run of the actual shipped module code end-to-end (save→session builder→add recipe→schedule preview→Accept→agenda push), zero JS errors. Full record: `records/2026-09/habits_cooking_domain_interrogation_spec_2026-09-10.txt` section 10 + `patch_notes.html`'s Sep 13 card. **Real, deliberate scope line held**: plain date/time only, no shift/calendar collision-checking — that's H5, still real, specified, and **not yet built** (deferred, no change this pass). **Untested by Alex's own hand** — same standing flag as the rest of this domain. **H7/H8/H9 shipped Sep 13 2026, same day, real CEO Loop 1 (`records/2026-09/habits_shopping_pantry_calories_spec_2026-09-13.txt`) → Loop 2, Alex's own explicit "log both questions as blue for later, do you to h9."** Real new tables: `pantry_stock` (a genuine persistent stock table, not a per-list tick), `shopping_lists`/`shopping_list_items` — all `anon_read_only`/`authenticated_all`. `cookingOracle` gained a real "📦 Pantry" view/edit, a "🛒 Generate shopping list" have/not-have split computed against live pantry stock from a planned-cook session, a price-entry popup on marking an item bought (real "↺ Redo" to correct before it's final) writing plain inserts into `ingredient_prices` (never updates, so price history accumulates naturally — brand/store columns added), and a recipe-card "🔎 Estimate Nutrition" section (kcal/protein/carbs/fat, totals + per-serving) using cached per-ingredient nutrition first, falling back to a live lookup. Oracle's `RECIPE_JSON:` trailer extended with a per-ingredient `grams_estimate`. **Real, same-day correction — Alex flagged the USDA choice directly ("Maybe not USDA, as I am in the uk"): USDA FoodData Central is a US database.** Real web evidence gathered before swapping (never guessed): CoFID (McCance & Widdowson, the UK government's own equivalent) has no live official API, only a static ~3,300-row downloadable dataset — a real future import job, not built this pass. Alex confirmed "Both, staged": `api/oracle.js`'s `lookupUsdaNutrition`/`action:'usda-lookup'` fully replaced (not left dormant alongside) with `lookupOpenFoodFactsNutrition`/`action:'nutrition-lookup'` — [Open Food Facts](https://uk.openfoodfacts.org), free, genuinely **no API key at all** (its own stated policy: read calls need only a descriptive `User-Agent`, no signup), live now, no "not configured" gate needed. Real UK preference: among up to 5 search matches, prefers one tagged `en:united-kingdom` in `countries_tags`, else first result. Real, honest tradeoff kept, not hidden: Open Food Facts is a crowdsourced BRANDED-PRODUCT database, so a generic ingredient name ("onion") can match a packaged product's nutrition rather than a true raw value. **Stage 2 (CoFID) built same session, same day, Alex: "Now do cofid"** — a new `cofid_foods` table (trigram-indexed `food_name`) + `cookingOracle.logic._lookupCofid` (client-side, zero AI call/server round-trip) now checked FIRST in `_ensureNutrition`, before Open Food Facts. Real word-overlap scoring picks the best `ilike`-matched candidate by counting overlapping words (never a bare substring accept); a real edge case (a CoFID row with every nutrient null, e.g. "Water, tap") is explicitly excluded from winning — caught and fixed via a synthetic-fixture test before shipping, not after. **Real, superseding update, same session, same day: the "genuinely empty" gap above is CLOSED.** `scripts/import_cofid.py` (header auto-detection, fuzzy column matching, correct `Tr`/`N`/footnote-marker handling) worked exactly as tested. Alex delivered the real files directly as chat attachments (the official 2021 XLSX + a genuinely separate "CoFID_oldFoods.xlsx" legacy dataset + the PDF docs) in response to the concrete gov.uk-download ask below — real inspection confirmed zero food-code overlap between the two files (2886 vs 393 unique codes), so both were imported, not just one: 3,280 real UK foods now live in `cofid_foods` (2887 `source='cofid'`, 393 `source='cofid_legacy'`). **A real bug was caught and fixed before import, not after**: `match_column()`'s multi-hint logic used `all()` instead of `any()`, silently discarding `food_group` for every row (the real header is bare "Group," never the literal phrase "food group") — fixed and re-verified before running the real batch. `_lookupCofid` now wins real matches ahead of Open Food Facts for generic ingredients, spot-checked against "chicken breast"/"onion"/"rice, white, basmati" with correct results. `ceo_plan_items` H9-COFID moved yellow→green. Untested by Alex's own hand in the live recipe UI. (The rest of this paragraph, including the gov.uk-download ask, describes the real state AT THE TIME it was written — kept per this file's own doc-discipline, superseded by the correction above.) `nutrition_source` now stores `'openfoodfacts'`; the old `noKey` concept is gone from `_ensureNutrition`'s return shape, replaced with an honest `lookupFailed` flag for a real transient miss (network/rate-limit), surfaced as an inline caveat rather than a separate blocking message. **Real, deliberate scope line held**: recipe-level nutrition only, no daily calorie log — deferred until Alex is back at the gym post-hand-injury. **Two real, pre-existing bugs found and fixed in the same pass, not shipped around**: (1) H6's own `_showSchedulePreview` (and this session's first draft of the new shopping-list generator) built a PostgREST `in.()` filter by wrapping each UUID in literal double quotes before joining — an invalid URL, throwing "Failed to fetch" before any real network attempt; fixed to the established unquoted-join convention already used elsewhere (`taxonomy_tree`/`bibliography`/etc). **H6's own Sep 13 "verified via headless Chromium, zero JS errors" claim never actually exercised this code path** — corrected here, not silently. (2) `RPGACE.sb.select`'s cache wrapper was missing `pantry_stock` from its `noCache` list, letting a fresh pantry write hide behind a stale ≤60s cached read and corrupt the have/not-have split — fixed, matching the existing `rpgace_shifts` precedent. Verified via a real headless-Chromium Playwright harness (19/19 assertions) after fixing a Service-Worker-interception issue in the TEST HARNESS itself (`serviceWorkers:'block'` needed on the browser context, since `rpgace_core.js`'s real `sw.js` registration otherwise intercepts every GET before `page.route()` sees it). `node --check` clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped `20260912a`→`20260913a`. **One fork resolved same day (nutrition provider — Open Food Facts, see the correction above); one still deliberately deferred**: unit-to-gram normalization for ingredients with no cached weight — logged blue, not built. **Untested by Alex's own hand.** **Real fix, Sep 13 2026, same day, later pass — Alex's own direct complaint: "When i say in text field something Korean, I'm not given a list of 5 to choose from as discussed, I want a range or questions asking particular ingredients, or what type of dish (soup, noodle, rice, starter or dessert etc."** Real evidence checked first (rule 1): `cookingOracle.logic._generate` sent exactly one Oracle call for exactly one recipe with zero disambiguation, and no prior spec file (`records/2026-09/habits_cooking_domain_interrogation_spec_2026-09-10.txt` or elsewhere in `records/`) actually documents a "list of 5" agreement — treated as a genuine new gap, not a lost feature. Fixed: a description of 3 words or fewer now opens a real "Narrow it down" step (`ui._showNarrowDown`) instead of generating blind — 8 dish-type chips (Soup/Noodle/Rice/Main/Starter/Dessert/Side/Drink), an optional ingredients field, and an on-demand "🎲 Suggest 5 ideas" button (one real, cheap Oracle call, never a second premium-model triage call ahead of the real generation call, per rule 11); picking an idea or pressing "Generate with this info" runs the normal recipe generation. A description longer than 3 words is unaffected. A real rule-8 dedup landed alongside it: the original Generate button's disable/spin/call/show-card sequence was extracted into one shared `ui._runGenerateAndShow`, now used by all 3 real exit paths instead of each re-implementing its own copy. **Real, superseding correction, same day, immediate follow-up — a real live-production bug, found from Alex's own screenshot ("Could not parse dish ideas from the reply"), the moment he actually tried Korean → Rice → "Cabbage."** Real root cause, found by reading the actual capture pipeline, not guessed: the prompt originally asked for a numbered list, but `oracle:response-scanned`'s `text` is `lastMsg.textContent` read AFTER `renderMarkdown()` has already rendered the reply — `renderMarkdown()`'s own `^\d+\. ` regex strips a numbered list's digit prefix outright, and DOM `textContent` never reinserts a newline for the block-display markup that separated the original list items, so a numbered/bulleted reply is genuinely unrecoverable by the time ANY consumer sees it. Every prior consumer of this hook (`RECIPE_JSON:`/`DIRECTOR_CHOSEN:`/`EDL_JSON:`/`ORACLE_ACTION:`) only ever hunts one inline marker followed by a single blob, never multiple distinct list items — this feature is the first consumer needing real list extraction, exactly why it's the first to hit this. **A 2nd real bug found in the same pass**: `addMsg`'s own AI-message markup (`<div class="ai-name">✦ Oracle</div>${renderMarkdown(text)}`) has no separator, so `textContent` glues "✦ Oracle" directly onto the real reply — no prior consumer noticed since none of them split from the very start of the string. **Fixed at the prompt level, not patched around**: Oracle is now asked for one pipe-delimited line ("Dish One | Dish Two | ...") instead of a numbered list — survives both facts above; `_parseDishList` strips the known "✦ Oracle" label prefix, defensively strips a stray leading number/bullet per piece, and treats a single unbroken >80-char result as a genuine parse failure rather than silently presenting a garbled "idea." **Real, honest process finding**: the first ship's own "8/8 real assertions" headless test stubbed `visualOracle._captureNextResponse` directly with a hand-typed callback — it never actually rendered a reply through the real `addMsg`→`renderMarkdown`→DOM→`textContent` pipeline, which is exactly how both bugs slipped through verification and shipped anyway. Re-verified via a real headless-Chromium test that calls the real `addMsg()` and reads back what `lastMsg.textContent` actually contains — reproduces both bugs exactly as Alex saw them, then proves the fixed prompt+parser recovers all 5 real ideas through that same real pipeline (all 8 original assertions + 4 new real-capture-path assertions pass, zero console errors). `node --check` clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped `20260913f`→`20260913i`. **Untested by Alex's own hand yet on this specific fix.** **Real follow-up, Sep 13 2026, same day, immediate — Alex confirmed the fix worked (a real "Tom Yum Cabbage Soup" recipe generated correctly) then raised 2 more real gaps verbatim: "no option to add or suggest ingredients (like meat, love meat, or fish) also don't see the option to add it to current planned cook for grocery list."** Real `/interrogation` (AskUserQuestion, both answered) confirmed scope before building: (1) **both** a manual add row and Oracle-suggested additions, explicitly **pantry-aware "so waste is reduced"** (Alex's own words); (2) the existing automatic "Save silently adds to the in-progress cook session" behavior gets a **real, separate, visible button** alongside it, not just a relabel. Built: the recipe card gained a plain manual "+ Add ingredient" row (name/amount/unit, no Oracle call, free) that also correctly resets the nutrition estimate to "not yet looked up" since the total is now stale; a "💡 Suggest additions (uses your pantry)" button firing a real, separate Oracle call — a new `INGREDIENT_SUGGESTIONS_JSON:` trailer, same compact-single-line-JSON convention `RECIPE_JSON:` already uses (never a numbered/bulleted list, per the standing landmine below) — reading the recipe's current ingredients plus real `pantry_stock` (a new `logic._loadPantryNamed` helper, factored out of `ui._showPantry`'s own inline query per rule 8, now shared by both) and asking Oracle to prefer a pantry item when it genuinely fits, to reduce food waste; each suggestion shows a real, **code-computed** "📦 in your pantry" tag (checked against the actual pantry rows already in hand, never trusted from the model's own claim, rule 7) and its own "+ Add" button. A new, explicit "📅 Add to Cook Session" button now sits next to "💾 Save to Journal" — Save's own existing automatic add-to-session behavior is unchanged (no regression); the new button is a 2nd real, visible path to the same H6 session builder for someone who hasn't clicked Save yet, saving the recipe first only if it hasn't been saved already (a `savedRecipeId` closure guard prevents a 2nd duplicate `recipes` row on a later click of either button). Verified via a real headless-Chromium test exercising the actual capture pipeline again (`addMsg`→`renderMarkdown`→DOM→`textContent`, not a mocked callback) — 6/6 real assertions pass, zero console/page errors from the app's own code (the only console errors were pre-blocked external network calls the test sandbox itself blocks, e.g. Google Fonts/Supabase boot reads — confirmed unrelated by direct inspection). `node --check` clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped `20260913i`→`20260913j`. **Untested by Alex's own hand yet.** **Real, superseding hand-test follow-up, same day, immediate — Alex actually clicked both buttons and got both a journal save AND a session add from one recipe, then said "always do dedup" and asked to make the two buttons mean genuinely different things: "make so that save to cook just saves to cook, but not to journal, so that I can then rate the dishes and save liked ones."** Real root cause: `_save` always wrote a journal row internally regardless of which button called it, so "Add to Cook Session" was silently creating one too — and a fast click of both buttons in quick succession (or a same-button double-tap) could fire 2 concurrent saves, since each button only checked a flag (`savedRecipeId`), never a real in-flight guard. **Fixed with one shared `ensureRecipeSaved` helper both buttons now call** — it queues a second caller instead of starting a second real save whenever one is already in flight, so the `recipes` row is only ever inserted once no matter the click pattern (real dedup, per rule 8). `_save` gained a real `withJournal` flag: "Add to Cook Session" never writes a journal entry now; "Save to Journal" always does, via a new `_insertJournalEntry` (reused whether the recipe was already saved by the other button first, or not). **Same session, a third real Alex ask**: "clicking Dom module should also give way to a pop up for buttons to access either planned cook, grocery list planned or recipe generator." The dashDeck Cooking card's `go:` now opens a real 3-button hub (`ui._showModuleHub` — Planned Cook / Grocery List / Recipe Generator) instead of jumping straight into resume-session-or-generate; Grocery List checks the real `shopping_lists` table directly (never claims "none exists" just because the in-memory session hasn't rebuilt one yet on this page load). Verified via a real headless-Chromium test exercising the actual dedup path — a genuine concurrent double-click across both buttons, and a same-button rapid double-click, plus the new hub's 3 entry points and its honest empty-state fallback — 6/6 new assertions pass, no regression on the prior 6. `node --check` clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped `20260913j`→`20260913k`. **Untested by Alex's own hand yet.** **Real hand-test follow-up, Sep 14 2026, next day — Alex actually generated a recipe and reported 2 real problems, verbatim: "the method is poor, why prep seafood before anything when it is added at the end? Prep should be for everything that is part of active cook without beforehand dead time, so why do this? I want oracle float button to be able to take criticisms while I'm on cooking module so I can discuss what I want changed, would be huge."** (1) **Prompt fix**: the real root cause was the generation prompt letting the model dump every ingredient's prep into one opening block regardless of when it's actually used — raw seafood was being prepped 20+ minutes before it's added, a real food-safety/freshness issue, not just an awkward flow. Fixed at the prompt level, not patched around: `_generate`'s prompt now explicitly tells Oracle to sequence prep like an experienced cook — only front-load ingredients that genuinely hold up sitting prepped (vegetables/aromatics/dry goods/marinating meat), and give anything quick-to-prep and used near the END of cooking (raw seafood/fish especially, delicate garnishes) its own separate step positioned shortly before it's actually used, never bundled into the opening block. (2) **Context-aware Oracle overlay, real new architecture**: the floating 🔮 overlay button (`oracleControl`, already reachable from any page since G41) had zero awareness of what was on screen. Built a real, generic `registerContextProvider(key, fn)` registry on `oracleControl` (rule 8 — a shared mechanism any module can plug into, not a Cooking-specific hack) — `cookingOracle` registers a provider returning the currently-open recipe's real title/ingredients/method, gated on a real `_recipeCardOpen` flag (set true when the card opens, cleared via the popup's own `onClose` — never a stale reference lingering after the card closes). The overlay now silently prepends this real context to the text actually sent to Oracle, while the visible chat bubble still shows Alex's own exact typed words unchanged — so "the method is poor, why prep seafood before anything" lands already knowing which specific recipe/method it refers to, without him having to restate it. Verified via a real headless-Chromium test: the generation prompt carries the new sequencing rules; no recipe open → zero context prefix; a recipe open → the real sent text carries title/ingredients/method while the visible bubble stays exactly what was typed; closing the card correctly clears the context. 5/5 assertions pass, zero console errors. `node --check` clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped `20260913k`→`20260914a`. **Untested by Alex's own hand yet.** **Real "close the loop" follow-up, same day, immediate — Alex asked directly whether a critique typed into the overlay could actually change the open recipe card, not just be discussed: it could not, so this was built next.** Confirmed spec, Alex's own exact words: "Yes let me confirm changes first, but make sure only changes are shown, not the whole thing, so I can make a quick informed decision." Real design fork resolved per rule 5: Oracle returns the FULL revised recipe (reusing generation's own `RECIPE_JSON:` shape under a new `RECIPE_UPDATE_JSON:` trailer) and the client code-COMPUTES the diff against the real current recipe object (`cookingOracle.logic._diffRecipe`, ingredients matched by normalized name, steps by normalized description so a pure reorder never reads as delete-then-add) — never trusting Oracle's own self-reported account of what changed. A new, real PERSISTENT listener (`logic._listenForRecipeUpdates`, on the same shared `oracle:response-scanned` hook `DIRECTOR_CHOSEN:`/`EDL_JSON:`/`ORACLE_ACTION:` already use, rule 8 — deliberately not a one-shot capture like generation's own, since a critique can land as any later overlay message) only opens a real, distinct confirm popup (`ui._showRecipeUpdateConfirm`) when `diff.hasChanges` is genuinely true, showing ONLY the added/removed/changed ingredients and steps, never the whole recipe — matching Alex's literal words. Accept mutates the SAME live recipe object the open card's own closures already reference (a real `self._applyRecipeUpdate` function-to-module bridge, set while the card is open, cleared to `null` on close) and refreshes the ingredient list/step list/title IN PLACE — no popup close-reopen, so scroll position and any running step timer survive. `oracleControl._mirrorReplyToOverlay` also strips the raw `RECIPE_UPDATE_JSON:` trailer so the JSON blob never dumps into the visible overlay chat bubble. Verified via a real headless-Chromium test exercising the actual `RPGACE.hooks.fire` pipeline: 7/7 real assertions pass (context instruction present, diff-only popup on a real change, zero unchanged content shown, Accept refreshes the live card in place, raw trailer never leaks into the overlay bubble, a no-op update opens no popup, closing the card clears the apply-bridge so a late reply can't touch a closed card), zero real console/page errors. `node --check` clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped `20260914a`→`20260914b`. **Untested by Alex's own hand yet.**

- **Asian ingredients/nutrition expansion — real sourcing pass + a generalized regional-food importer built Sep 14 2026, zero data imported yet.** Alex's own ask: "I would like to add Asian products to the list of products and ingredients we have made, where is best to get a dataset for you?" Real, web-search-checked findings per region (South/Southeast/East Asia, all three confirmed in scope via AskUserQuestion): **India (IFCT 2017)** — official National Institute of Nutrition table, 528 foods, already re-published as a structured Kaggle CSV — the easiest source of the five. **Japan (MEXT)** — official Standard Tables of Food Composition, ~2,478 foods, real government Excel download with English food names — CoFID-shaped, no scraping needed. **Southeast Asia (ASEANFOODS/INFOODS)** — official, ~1,700 raw ingredients, PDF-only, no structured export found — a real future PDF-extraction job, not started. **Korea (RDA)** — a real official English search UI exists, no bulk download/API confirmed. **China (CFCT)** — no public download found at all; likely needs Alex to source it directly, same as CoFID's own real XLSX files arriving as chat attachments rather than a URL. **Real, confirmed blocker**: this session's own egress proxy blocks kaggle.com/*.github.io/mext.go.jp directly (same restriction class `import_cofid.py`'s own docstring already names for gov.uk/supabase.co) — not worked around, per standing policy. **Built anyway, real rule-8 dedup**: `scripts/import_food_composition.py`, a generalized sibling to `import_cofid.py` reusing its exact proven mechanism (fuzzy header detection, OR-hint column matching, fail-loud, batched SQL) for any regional source (.csv or .xlsx), writing into the SAME `cofid_foods` table under a new `source` tag — confirmed directly that `source` is a plain unconstrained `text` column, zero migration needed; `_lookupCofid` already scans the whole table regardless of source, so zero client code change is needed either once real rows land. Verified via 2 real synthetic fixtures (an IFCT-shaped CSV, a MEXT-shaped XLSX) before shipping — one real cosmetic bug (a wrong summary-path string) caught and fixed via the same test. `py_compile` clean. **Real, honest scope at the time this was written**: tooling only, ready to run the moment Alex provides a real IFCT/MEXT file — zero Asian ingredients actually in RPGACE yet. **This scope line is now stale — see the real, superseding update immediately below, same day.**

- **Real, superseding update, Sep 14 2026, same day, later pass — "Do korea and china too, dig deeper, and give me every website i need to visit for downloads." India and China are now genuinely imported, not just tooled-up.** Real, previously-unknown environment fact discovered while trying to satisfy the "give me every website" ask: `raw.githubusercontent.com` is reachable from this session's sandboxed egress even though `github.com`/`api.github.com`/`kaggle.com`/`unpkg.com`/`cdn.jsdelivr.net`/`data.go.kr` are all confirmed blocked (`403`/`connect_rejected`, direct `curl` tests) — a materially better outcome than the original ask, since it let real structured datasets be fetched and imported directly rather than only cataloged as links. **India (IFCT 2017)**: fetched `ifct2017/ifct2017`'s own real `compositions/index.csv` (542 real foods, 151 nutrient columns) via `raw.githubusercontent.com`. **Real IFCT unit bug caught before any insert, not shipped blind**: the file's own `enerc` column is genuinely in kilojoules, not kcal (real INFOODS convention — bare ENERC = kJ, only ENERC_KCAL = kcal), caught by a plausibility check (amaranth seed showing 1490 "kcal" — implausible for a raw cereal) and confirmed via a known-value cross-check (raw milled rice: 1491/4.184 = 356.4 kcal, matching its real ~356 kcal/100g) — fixed with a new `--energy-is-kj` flag (`/4.184` Atwater conversion) on the import script rather than shipped with the wrong unit. **A real, deliberate manual correction, disclosed here plainly per rule 7**: 14 real "Edible Oils and Fats" IFCT rows (coconut oil, ghee, corn oil, etc.) showed `enerc=0` in the raw source despite being ~100% fat — a genuine gap in the source data, not a parsing bug. Rather than import a technically-accurate-but-actively-misleading `kcal_per_100g=0` for pure oils (which would silently zero out any recipe's oil-calorie contribution, since `_lookupCofid` only excludes NULL kcal, never zero), these 14 rows were manually overridden to `884.0` kcal (the standard Atwater fat-energy constant, 100g × 8.84 kcal/g) before insert — a high-confidence correction grounded in a universal physical constant, not a guess, but a real deviation from the raw imported file, named here so it's checkable. **China (CFCT)**: fetched `Sanotsu/china-food-composition-data`'s real, community-compiled CSV (1677 rows, 1241 with real usable English names) via the same `raw.githubusercontent.com` path — needed `--name-col englishName` (the shared hint list would have wrongly matched the Chinese-language `foodName` column) and `--carbs-col CHO` (an explicit override built specifically to avoid a bare "cho" hint risking a false match against "cholesterol"). `parse_number()` needed zero changes — its existing regex fallback already correctly handles China's real missing-value tokens (`'Tr'`/`'—'`/`'un'`) and footnote-marked values (`'899*'` → 899.0). **Real, live-verified final counts** (`select source, count(*) from cofid_foods group by source`): `cofid`=2887, `cofid_legacy`=393, `ifct_india`=542, `cfcd_china`=1241 — **5063 total real foods now in `cofid_foods`**, zero client code changes needed (`_lookupCofid` already scans the whole table regardless of `source`). **One real, small, honestly-flagged discrepancy**: the China source had 1242 usable rows at extraction time but only 1241 landed — a genuine off-by-one not tracked down further this pass (proportionate to its size, rule 11), not silently smoothed over. **Korea and Japan stay real website-only gaps, dug deeper as asked but not imported** — Korea: no GitHub-hosted structured mirror exists; the real official path is MFDS's Open API (`data.go.kr`, item id 15127578, base `apis.data.go.kr/1471000/FoodNtrCpntDbInfo02`, needs free registration) or the RDA's English search UI (`koreanfood.rda.go.kr/eng/fctFoodSrchEng/engMain`), both blocked from direct fetch by this session's own egress. Japan: a real GitHub JSON mirror exists (`katoharu432/standards-tables-of-food-composition-in-japan`) but is Japanese-language-only with no English food names, unusable for RPGACE's English-word `_lookupCofid` matching without a real translation step not attempted this pass — the real recommended path is MEXT's own official English-paged 2015 Excel table (mext.go.jp, confirmed blocked). Southeast Asia stays PDF-only (ASEANFOODS, `inmu2.mahidol.ac.th/aseanfoods/composition_data.html`), same as originally found. `scripts/import_food_composition.py` gained real, disclosed extensions this pass — `--name-col`/`--group-col`/`--kcal-col`/`--protein-col`/`--carbs-col`/`--fat-col` explicit column overrides (for when the shared fuzzy-hint list is unsafe) and `--energy-is-kj` (the real kJ→kcal fix above) — `find_header_row()` also extended to search on an override column, not just the shared hint list, so header-ROW detection still succeeds when the real name column is too generic for the hints (IFCT's bare `name`). **Untested by Alex's own hand** — same standing flag as every prior CoFID-family import.

- **Real, superseding update, Sep 14 2026 (continuation session) — Japan (MEXT) and Southeast Asia (ASEAN FCD) both genuinely imported now, both real Alex file attachments (chat uploads, not fetched links).** Alex provided the real official MEXT 2015 Standard Tables of Food Composition (Seventh Revised Edition) xlsx and the real official ASEANFOODS FCD V1 2014 PDF directly. **Japan (MEXT)**: the xlsx needed a real preprocessing step before `import_food_composition.py` could touch it (`scripts/prep_mext.py`, one-time, not a generalized script) — mapped 18 real, web-search-verified official MEXT food-group codes to English names, and stripped MEXT's own documented parenthesis-estimated-value convention (`"(12.5)"` = a real calculated/estimated value, not missing). **Real data-quality issue found and excluded, not imported blind**: one row had a placeholder food name (`'－'`, a bare full-width dash) — confirmed via a full-dataset scan as the only such row across 2191, excluded rather than guessed at. Final verified count: `select count(*) from cofid_foods where source='mext_japan'` = **2190**. **Southeast Asia (ASEAN FCD)**: real, harder extraction — plain-text PDF extraction (`pypdf`/`pdfminer`) was confirmed via direct spot-check to GARBLE adjacent numeric table cells with no space between glyphs (e.g. a real "557 2.2 23.4" rendered as "5 5 72 . 2 2 3 . 4"), a genuine silent-corruption risk if used blind — switched to `pdfplumber`'s character-position-based table extraction instead, which correctly isolated the same cells cleanly, verified by direct comparison on the same row. `scripts/extract_asean.py` (new, ASEAN-specific, not a generalized script — the PDF's own 26-column table with 3 space-joined 7-value blob cells is a one-off shape) parses "The Concise ASEAN Food Composition Tables" (PDF pages 34-66 of the "Electronic version 1, February 2014" edition), resolves the real documented `<INFOODS>` tagname convention (parens = estimated value, `T`/`Tr` = trace, `-` = no analysis, and a real, initially-mishandled `Np` suffix meaning "presumed zero" — confirmed from the PDF's own page-30 "Signs, symbols and abbreviations" key, fixed before shipping since it was silently dropping ~62 real, legitimate zero-carb values as missing). **A real, honest scope finding, disclosed not smoothed over**: the source text (page 21) describes the original 2000 print edition as having "1,746 food items" — this specific 2014 electronic PDF genuinely contains far fewer, cross-validated by two independent methods (table-cell extraction and a raw-text food-ID regex scan, both converging on ~610-620 real distinct items) — this is a real property of the specific PDF Alex provided, not an extraction bug, and is stated here plainly rather than the smaller real count being quietly presented as if it were the full 1,746. **A real, small hand-recovery pass, disclosed**: 8 rows were genuinely unrecoverable by the automated table extraction (a rare pdfplumber column-merge artifact on those specific rows) — the raw text for each was inspected directly; 5 had fully clean, legible values and were hand-entered (Duck blood/Grouper/Sheat-fish/Jelly w: coconut cream/Toddy palm in syrup), 1 more (Amaranth) and 2 more (Chicken thigh/wing) were recovered from a separate blob-token-count anomaly the same way; 3 rows (2 with garbled/unrecoverable food names, 1 with only ambiguous partial values and nothing of real substance) were excluded rather than guessed. Final verified count: `select count(*) from cofid_foods where source='asean_fcd'` = **612**. **Real, live-verified grand total across all 6 sources** (`select source, count(*) from cofid_foods group by source`): `cofid`=2887, `cofid_legacy`=393, `ifct_india`=542, `cfcd_china`=1241, `mext_japan`=2190, `asean_fcd`=612 — **7865 total real foods now in `cofid_foods`**, zero client code changes needed (`_lookupCofid` already scans the whole table regardless of `source`). **Korea stays the one remaining real gap** — same finding as before this pass, no structured source found, MFDS's Open API / RDA's English search UI both blocked from this session's egress; would need Alex's own hand the same way MEXT/ASEAN's raw files did. `node --check` clean on both new scripts (`py_compile`). **Untested by Alex's own hand** — same standing flag as every prior CoFID-family import; this pass adds a genuinely new source-quality risk class (PDF table-cell extraction) worth a real spot-check in the live recipe UI before fully trusting it at scale.

- **Real, superseding update, Sep 14 2026 (same continuation session, immediate follow-up) — real cross-source deduplication run against all 6 `cofid_foods` sources, Alex's own direct ask: "I would like all these rows to be deduplicated so all ingredients have one row with all columns in tact."** Real interrogation first (2 forks, both AskUserQuestion-confirmed on the recommended option) rather than guessing at a genuinely consequential, destructive-if-wrong request against a table with **zero Supabase backup/PITR**: (1) match rule = exact name match only (case/whitespace-insensitive), never a fuzzy/AI-judged "conceptually the same food" match — different regional cultivars/prep states of, say, "rice" genuinely have different real nutrition, and a wrong fuzzy merge would silently corrupt data with no way to recover it; (2) conflict rule = keep ONE authoritative row per duplicate name, but preserve every other region's real values rather than discard or average them. **Real evidence gathered before touching anything**: exact-name grouping (case/whitespace-insensitive) found **109 real duplicate groups, 231 rows involved, 122 rows to remove** (~3% of the then-7865-row table) — mostly genuine same-name overlaps across regions (e.g. "Honey" in both `cofid` and `mext_japan`: 288 vs 294 kcal — real, both correct, genuinely different honeys). **Built**: a new, purely additive `cofid_foods.regional_variants` jsonb column (zero destructive schema change) holds a real array of every non-winning region's full row (`source`/`food_name`/`food_group`/`kcal_per_100g`/`protein_g_per_100g`/`carbs_g_per_100g`/`fat_g_per_100g`) — literally "all columns in tact," per Alex's own words, just consolidated onto one canonical row instead of scattered across several. Winner selection uses a real, disclosed source-priority order (`cofid` > `cofid_legacy` > `ifct_india` > `mext_japan` > `cfcd_china` > `asean_fcd` — UK-official first since Alex is UK-based, then other official-government tables, community-compiled and older regional-compilation sources last) — a genuine judgment call, but a low-stakes one, since nothing is actually lost either way: the "wrong" pick still keeps every real value via `regional_variants`. **Verified at every step, not assumed**: the UPDATE (winner ← losers as `regional_variants`) confirmed exactly 109 rows tagged, matching the 109 real groups; a direct spot-check on "Honey" confirmed the real MEXT values (294 kcal, 0.2g protein, 79.7g carbs, 0g fat) landed correctly on the `cofid` winner row; the DELETE removed exactly 122 rows (7865→**7743**, matching the pre-computed count exactly); a fresh post-delete duplicate scan confirmed **zero remaining exact-name duplicate groups**. Real final per-source counts: `cofid`=2885 (-2), `cofid_legacy`=393 (unchanged), `ifct_india`=529 (-13), `cfcd_china`=1185 (-56), `mext_japan`=2174 (-16), `asean_fcd`=577 (-35) — losses sum to exactly 122. **Zero client code changes needed** — `_lookupCofid` reads `kcal_per_100g`/`protein_g_per_100g`/`carbs_g_per_100g`/`fat_g_per_100g` from whichever row it matches; the new column is purely additive and inert to every existing read path. **Real, honest scope note**: this catches only EXACT-name duplicates — a genuinely different-but-conceptually-same food (e.g. "Rice, white, polished, raw" vs "Milled rice, raw") is deliberately left as two separate real rows, per the confirmed match-rule fork above, since collapsing those would risk exactly the silent-nutrition-corruption Alex's own request was trying to avoid, not cause. **Untested by Alex's own hand** — same standing flag as the rest of this import effort; `regional_variants` itself is not yet surfaced anywhere in the Cooking UI (a real future nice-to-have, not built this pass — Alex didn't ask for it to be shown, only for the data not to be lost).

- **Real, superseding update, Sep 14 2026 (same continuation session, immediate follow-up again) — real sub-grouping columns added, Alex's own direct ask: "Now deduplicate food names by how they are cooked, brand etc as sub groups to make values in one area for ease of navigation."** A genuinely different operation from the dedup bullet immediately above — this is non-destructive categorization, never another merge; zero rows removed, zero rows changed shape. Real 2-round `/interrogation` (both AskUserQuestion) resolved scope before touching data: (1) data-layer grouping columns only, not a new browsable ingredient-picker UI (Alex didn't ask for new UI, only "ease of navigation" of the data itself); (2) brand — real evidence checked first: none of the 6 government/regional food-composition sources (CoFID/IFCT/MEXT/CFCD/ASEAN FCD/cofid_legacy) carry real brand data at all, only a separate, unrelated Open Food Facts fallback table does — Alex confirmed "1 for now, then brands if info is there," i.e. skip brand population now, revisit only once a real brand-bearing source exists. **Built**: 2 new purely additive columns, `base_ingredient` (mechanical, `trim(split_part(replace(food_name, '，', ','), ',', 1))` — handles both ASCII and the real full-width Chinese comma seen in `cfcd_china` rows) and `prep_state` (mechanical, a 30-branch priority-ordered `CASE`/Postgres word-boundary regex, compound/specific cooking terms — stir-fried/deep-fried/pan-fried/freeze-dried/sun-dried/oven-dried — checked before their generic counterparts, per this project's own standing "hyphens are boundaries, keyword lists need compound phrases" landmine discipline) plus a 3rd column, `brand` (schema added, deliberately left NULL project-wide — no real source data exists to populate it yet, not a bug). Zero AI/Oracle calls (rule 11 — purely mechanical SQL text extraction, no judgment call needed for either field). **Verified at every step**: a dry-run count matched the real post-UPDATE count exactly (7743/7743 rows tagged with `base_ingredient`, 4391/7743 = 56.7% tagged with a real `prep_state`); a live "Chicken" spot-check confirmed correct grouping and full-width-comma handling across sources (e.g. `cfcd_china`'s `Chicken，braised，whole` correctly extracted `base_ingredient='Chicken'`/`prep_state='braised'`). **Real, honest scope note, not hidden**: `prep_state` is deliberately left NULL for the other 43.3% of rows rather than defaulted to `'raw'` — many of those rows are processed-but-unstated items (flour, oil, spices, whole nuts) where guessing "raw" would be actively wrong; a food_group breakdown confirmed this spans every group, a real property of these sources' own naming conventions, not a coverage bug. `idx_cofid_foods_base_ingredient`/`idx_cofid_foods_prep_state` indexes added alongside. Zero client code changes needed — `_lookupCofid` is untouched, these columns are purely additive and inert to every existing read path. **Untested by Alex's own hand** — same standing flag as the rest of this import effort; no UI currently surfaces `base_ingredient`/`prep_state` browsing (real future nice-to-have, not built this pass, per the confirmed data-layer-only scope).

- **Aug 22 2026 — the real `boot_load_lag_client_interactive_12000ms` root cause found and fixed, via Alex's own successfully-captured DevTools Performance flame chart, closing the "do this that you sent me" ask directly.** Alex hand-captured a full 33.44s Performance recording (after several false starts — DevTools' "Record and reload" auto-stops on an idle heuristic well before RPGACE's delayed, login-triggered freeze occurs; the working method was: plain Record button → manual address-bar reload → log in → watch console for the `perfWatch` warning → manual stop). The flame chart's own call stack, read directly off his screenshot, showed the real 12-13 second red "Task" block under `refreshEncyclopediaDisplay → renderEncEntries → sortEntries` — a genuinely different root cause than either of the two hypotheses this project had been chasing (network-request volume, boot-task staggering), both honestly ruled out earlier the same day by offline reproduction. Reading `sortEntries()` (line 3103) confirmed the real bug: its `'unique'` sort mode recomputed a full O(n) cross-entry text-overlap score from scratch **inside the sort comparator**, meaning the same expensive scan reran on every single comparison during the sort (`O(n log n)` full recomputations, not one) — and `let ENC_SORT = 'unique'` (line 3057) is the actual DEFAULT value, so this ran unconditionally on the Encyclopedia's very first render, not on some rare mode a user has to opt into. **Fixed, 100% behavior-preserving**: each entry's overlap score is now precomputed exactly once into a `Map` before the sort runs, and the comparator just reads from it — same output ordering, same content, just computed once instead of ~n times. A separate, pre-existing correctness bug in the same original algorithm (a case-sensitivity inconsistency — `allText` was never lowercased while the per-entry comparison text was, silently breaking both the self-exclusion filter and the substring match) was found in passing and **deliberately left unfixed** to keep this a pure performance fix with zero behavior change — flagged here as a real, separate, open item for Alex's own call, not silently bundled in. **Quantified before shipping, not just asserted (rule 1/4 discipline)**: a standalone Node.js benchmark (old vs. new, both algorithms extracted verbatim) confirmed a consistent real **2.0x speedup** at every tested size (n=300: 2051ms→1003ms; n=500: 5426ms→2741ms), and at content length matching the real ~3000-char Oracle-prompt slice, the OLD algorithm's true severity became visible: n=300 took 9.5s, n=500 took 27s, n=800 took **68.8s** (all fixed at exactly 2.0x, so NEW = ~4.8s/13.4s/33.6s respectively) — a clean `O(n²)` scaling curve (each size-ratio squared predicts the measured time almost exactly), confirming this wasn't just "slow," it was genuinely capable of freezing the tab for over a minute at a large-enough real Encyclopedia, far past the ~12s Alex actually hit. **Honest caveats, not smoothed over**: the benchmark uses synthetic entries — Alex's own real-world re-test after this deploys is the true confirmation of how much better it feels; and the `'unique'` mode remains inherently `O(n²)`-in-entry-count even after this fix (just with the constant factor cut in half) — whether `ENC_SORT`'s default should move off `'unique'` entirely as a further safety margin is a real, undecided, unforced question for Alex, not acted on here. `node --check` clean, re-minified (`rpgace_core.min.js` regenerated), cache-bust bumped `20260822e`→`20260822f`. `error_log` row `aebddd95-3a2b-4e20-9a07-8693f6fdbee8` updated with this final root cause.

dropped from a repeatedly-measured ~12,150-13,329ms down to a single real `perfWatch` warning of **1,404ms** (~89% real reduction, a full order-of-magnitude change), confirming `sortEntries` really The real underlying gap WAS genuine though: `errorLog` only ever caught `window.onerror`/`unhandledrejection`, so a handled validation toast (e.g. "Add a beat title first") was invisible to `error_log` entirely. **Step 1 (commit `408e2f4`)**: `toast()` now writes error-colored (`#CC4A4A`, the codebase's own established error convention — confirmed via grep, 33 of ~80 real call sites) toasts into `error_log` via `errorLog._capture`, and fires a new `rpgace:error-toast` hook; `oracleControl` buffers the last 5 and surfaces them to Oracle via a new `buildRecentErrorsBlock()` on the existing `TRIGGER_KEYWORDS` gate. **Step 2, the bigger ask, resolved via 3 real `AskUserQuestion` forks rather than guessed**: Alex confirmed (1) extend `/perspective`'s existing 44-row per-module `expected_behavior` baseline, not a new learning mechanism; (2) `smoke_test_items` (which already has real `broken`/`linked_error_id`/`linked_plan_item_id` columns) is the real trigger surface — flips to `broken` automatically, cascades to `error_log` and, only from a genuine prior-`green` state, to `ceo_plan_items.status='purple'`, matching `/colourgradient`'s own existing regression definition exactly; (3) `error_log` itself is the real async queue, tied into a new CLAUDE.md session-start-check addendum + `/Routine`. **Real technical finding along the way**: a stack-trace-based module attribution does NOT work as first assumed — `RPGACE.register()` modules are plain object literals, so V8 reports a real error generically as `Object._submit`, with no module name at all (confirmed via a real headless Chromium test against the live minified app). Fixed with a new build-time generator, `scripts/generate_method_module_map.py` (real brace-depth parsing of every `RPGACE.register('name', {...})` block) — `mangle:false` (this project's permanent minify setting) preserves every real method name, so 432 of 456 real distinct method names resolve to exactly one owning module; the 24 genuinely ambiguous ones (`_inject`/`init`/`_render`/etc, shared by 2-8 modules) are correctly excluded, never guessed. `errorLog._capture` now accepts an optional stack, resolves it via `METHOD_MODULE_MAP`, attaches the matched module's real `perspective_reports` baseline (`linked_perspective_id`/`expected_baseline`) to a new `error_log` row (raw `fetch` + `Prefer:return=representation`, avoiding the standing `RPGACE.sb.insert()`-never-returns-the-id landmine, rule 8), and cascades to `smoke_test_items`/`ceo_plan_items` exactly as confirmed above. `future_integrations.html`'s own `#purple-live` section already live-queries `ceo_plan_items` for `status='purple'` — confirmed via direct read, zero changes needed there. Verified via headless Chromium with mocked Supabase network calls (real network is sandboxed here): the full cascade fired with correct request shapes at every step (dedup-check → perspective lookup → `error_log` insert → `smoke_test_items` broken-flip → `ceo_plan_items` purple-flip, correctly gated on the mocked prior `green` status), zero console errors; `_extractModule` separately confirmed `_submit`→`beatLog` and `_inject`→`null` (ambiguous, correctly rejected) against a real stack shape. `ceo_plan_items` G109 logged yellow. Full record: `records/2026-08/g109_error_perspective_smoketest_purple_loop_2026-08-26.txt`. **Untested against a real live error yet — mocked-network headless verification only.**

## HABITS domain narrative — collapsed from CLAUDE.md Sep 23 2026 (DD-1, real Oversight Doc Dedup & Compression pass)
