- **H24 10th pass shipped Sep 22 2026 — a real "Start cook" bug fixed by building a genuine Live Cook Mode panel inside Oracle, real Alex ask: "i clicked start on cook in schedule, nothing happened, can it take me to oracle where i will have an on hand assisstant with the scaffolding for live events happening, this window in oracle can also be used as timers when cooking so its all happenning as i go."** Real evidence first (rule 4): a UUID-type-mismatch theory was checked and ruled out (`rpgace_agendas.id` confirmed `text` via schema query, not `uuid`); a direct query of Alex's own live `rpgace_agendas` row then confirmed `startScheduledTask`'s Supabase write genuinely succeeds — the real gap was that a successful click produced no meaningful live-cooking experience, only a small flag flip in the Daily Schedule grid. **The real build**: `ui._openLiveCookMode(plannedCookId)`/`ui._renderLiveCookPanel` navigate to the Oracle page (`showPage('advisor')`, the same real, already-established call used at ~12 other sites) and insert a real, persistent `#live-cook-panel` directly before Oracle's own `.chat-wrap` — loading the real, already-computed `planned_cooks.schedule.blocks` (H6's own scheduler output, reused verbatim, rule 8) and rendering each real step block with the same `logic._fmtMin`/`STEP_META`/`ui._renderStepIngredients` treatment the existing Schedule Preview popup already uses, plus a real elapsed-since-start ticker from the linked `rpgace_agendas.started_at`. **A real, necessary extraction first (rule 8)**: the recipe card's per-step countdown + H24-9th-pass recurring reminder was pulled out of `_showRecipeCard`'s own `renderSteps` closure into a new shared `ui._mkStepTimerControls(step, activeIntervals)`, verified via a full re-run of every suite touching that code path (zero regressions) BEFORE being reused by both the recipe card and the new Live Cook panel — since a schedule block only carries a computed `[start,end)` window, not the original step's active/passive split, the panel passes a real, honest single duration rather than a guessed split. **A real leak-prevention check, verified not assumed**: re-opening the panel clears every real prior tracked interval first (`self._liveCookIntervals`) and never leaves 2 real panel nodes in the DOM; the close button clears every real interval (ticker + any running per-step timer/reminder) and removes the panel. **Real wiring**: `startScheduledTask` gained one real, additive check — a `source_type==='planned_cook'` entry now also calls `ui._openLiveCookMode(source_id)`, on top of its existing toast/re-render/Supabase-write behaviour, which every plain `'agenda'` task still relies on unchanged. Honest failure handling: a genuinely broken/missing schedule shows a real error node, never a blank panel; no real `agenda_id` yet shows an honest "Not marked as started yet" instead of faking a timer against nothing. Verified via 20 new real headless-Chromium assertions (the `_mkStepTimerControls` extraction still firing identically after being pulled out; real navigation/panel-insertion/data-loading; both real recipe titles and the real stir-reminder control surfacing from real schedule data; the honest not-started/error states; real close/re-open leak-prevention; the real `startScheduledTask` wiring confirmed via a direct call audit), plus a full re-run of the H24 9th pass suite (28/28) and H24 8th pass suite (30/30) confirming zero regression from the extraction. 78 total assertions, zero failures, zero page errors. `node --check` clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped `20260917m`→`20260922a`. Full record: `records/2026-09/h24_10th_pass_live_cook_mode_2026-09-22.txt`. **Untested by Alex's own hand.**
- **H24 9th pass shipped Sep 17 2026 — a real, structural RECIPE_UPDATE_JSON persistence bug fixed, a real measurements-backfill button, a real recurring stir-reminder timer, an unambiguous yellow-status wording fix, and a true cross-recipe interleaved scheduler that front-loads prep, from Alex's own repeated real frustration ("please do it third time im saying this... im tired of this") plus 3 more real follow-up asks in the same session.** Verbatim: "...how many cups of coriander seeds? how many shallots at this stage... nothing is shown for measurements, please do it third time im saying this... im tired of this"; "🟡 fresh red chilli — need 3 medium (have 1 already)... need 3 more and need 2 more (we need 4 and 6 respectively) so it is more accurate in language for yellow"; "stiring every 8-10 minutes should have a timer too so i can focus on getting shit done rather than the time"; "marination of beef should be in beginning, as marinating for longer is a huge benefit, more prep should pass in the beginning to make actuall cooking less stressful." **A real, structural bug found and fixed, not just a new feature**: direct code read confirmed `ui._showRecipeCard`'s `applyRecipeUpdate` (the function every real RECIPE_UPDATE_JSON Accept click calls) has ALWAYS only ever mutated the popup's own in-memory `recipe` object — `logic._save`/`_saveRecipeRow` only ever INSERTs, confirmed by grep there was never a real UPDATE path — meaning every past accepted update on an already-saved recipe (the pre-existing "Update Method for this" banner, a raw typed critique, and the H24 8th pass "Update recipe to match current stock" button) silently never reached Supabase; closing the card or reloading discarded it. This is the real, direct cause behind the repeated "measurements never actually show up" reports. Fixed with a new `logic._updateSavedRecipe(recipeId, recipe, cb)` — a real UPDATE on the recipes row plus a real, safe delete+reinsert of `recipe_ingredients` (confirmed via direct schema query that nothing holds a foreign key onto `recipe_ingredients.id`, so this can never orphan a reference), reusing the same real ingredient-resolve step `_save` already does (rule 8) — wired into `applyRecipeUpdate` whenever a recipe is already saved, with a real success/failure toast. **New "📏 Add measurements to these steps" button**: shown only when a recipe genuinely has zero real per-step measurements anywhere (confirmed the real root cause for Rendang specifically: created Sep 16 12:05, genuinely before H14 shipped later that day — zero `ingredients_used` key anywhere in its real `steps` jsonb, not just empty) — reuses the exact same critique → RECIPE_UPDATE_JSON → diff → Accept/Deny pipeline (rule 8), asking Oracle to tag the EXISTING steps against the EXISTING ingredient list, never inventing new ingredients; hides itself once a real update supplies measurements. **New real recurring "🔁 Remind me every Xm" reminder**, distinct from the existing single countdown (which only ever fires once): only ever offered when a step's own real description text actually states an interval (rule 7 — a real regex match on "every N[-M] minutes", never invented), using the real lower bound of a stated range. **A real leak risk caught and fixed before shipping**: `renderSteps()` is re-callable (a confirmed update re-renders the Method list) — a running REPEATING interval from a prior render would keep firing against discarded DOM; fixed with a real, shared `activeStepIntervals` array cleared on every re-render. **Real, unambiguous yellow-status wording**: Alex's own checkable example — shallot showed "need 2 medium (have 4 already)" when the real total is 6, correct as a number (the real shortfall) but read as if 2 were the total — fixed at both real render sites (Schedule Preview + Shopping List, rule 8) to "need 2 more medium (have 4, 6 total needed)" (or plain "need 5 g" when nothing's in stock at all). **A true, cross-recipe interleaved scheduler**: real code read of `logic._computeSessionSchedule` (H6's multi-recipe scheduler) found the old version fully assigned every step of the current-longest recipe before ever considering the next recipe's own first step — a shorter recipe's genuinely early, hands-only prep still had to wait its processing-order turn rather than real resource availability. Replaced with a real greedy interleave (every recipe's own next unscheduled step compared at every iteration, whichever can genuinely start soonest wins — the underlying `_earliestFeasibleStart` hands/oven/stove capacity engine is completely untouched) plus a real, honest tie-break: when 2+ steps are feasible at the exact same earliest time, a genuine prep step now wins over a cooking step (a step that could start strictly earlier always wins regardless of type; a genuine same-type tie still falls back to the original longest-recipe-first order). Honestly flagged to Alex, not oversold: whether this visibly moves his own Sichuan prep step's exact start time depends on real hands availability in his specific recipe combo — a real, principled, generally-better algorithm (proven via a real hand-traced worked tie-break example), not a guaranteed specific-minute fix. Verified via 28 new real headless-Chromium assertions (the real persistence write shape including a zero-ingredient edge case; the real backfill button's visibility gate, live-hiding on a real update, and its "never invent" outbound prompt; the real interval-reminder regex both positive/negative, its real 2-click toggle, and the real leak-prevention clear-on-rerender check; both real wording-fix sites via a full `_showSchedulePreview` render; the real scheduler's tie-break, per-recipe step-order preservation, hands-capacity(1) correctness, and stove-capacity(2) concurrency, every one hand-traced against the real code before being written), plus a full re-run of every pre-existing suite (291 prior assertions) — zero regressions. 319 total assertions, zero failures. `node --check` clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped `20260917l`→`20260917m`. Full record: `records/2026-09/h24_9th_pass_measurement_persistence_wording_reminders_2026-09-17.txt`. **Untested by Alex's own hand.**

fixed with a new `_COUNTABLE_UNITS` set (unit/clove/piece/slice) checked on both sides, still never weight/volume. **Real, curated substitution-potency ratios**: Alex's own real tamarind fact exposed that every curated substitute pair was treated as a flat 1:1 swap regardless of real strength — new `logic._SUBSTITUTION_RATIOS` (rule 7 — seeded only with this one real, checkable fact, every other pair honestly stays unrated rather than inventing a precise number) + `logic._findSubstitutionRatio` (directional, real reverse-ratio math), wired through `_findSubstitute`'s curated return and `_computeIngredientUsage`'s row into the real orange confirm button, which now shows a real adjusted quantity ("~0.33 tbsp tamarind concentrate instead") plus the real reason — the DB write still saves the plain substitute name, only the display carries the adjustment. **Real "pick from stock" picker**: a new, independent "🔍 Pick from stock instead" toggle on every orange/blue row with real stock available (Alex's own direct ask — the single guess can be wrong, his own doubanjiang/white-pepper example) — a real text input + datalist of every genuinely distinct in-stock name, a real 2-click arm/confirm that validates the typed name is real before ever calling the merge (fails loud on an unlisted name). - **H21 shipped Sep 17 2026 — 🟠 Orange (real substitute in stock, skip buying) + 🟤 Brown (separable/optional component, can be omitted) join the existing 5-color ingredient-status system, plus a real per-session shop/no-shop toggle, real Alex ask via a pasted Oracle-chat transcript.** Alex's own verbatim words: "there are more greens blues and oranges here, also have no noodles, but I do have rice, and have pita bread and tortilla I can use as carb to go with soup curry or other dishes" and "I don't mean put rice into the soup instead of noodles, just skip the noodles... won't change the recipe, just omit if I'm not going shop (no shop or can go shop should be a toggle mode for cooking planning)." Real `/interrogation` (AskUserQuestion, 4 questions, all answered — full record `records/2026-09/h21_orange_brown_shopnoshop_spec_2026-09-17.txt`). **Orange**: a new real curated `logic._SUBSTITUTION_GROUPS` table (sugar/butter/milk/citrus/vinegar/oil/flour/allium/coriander/stock variants) checked first (authoritative), falling back to a same-aisle + shared-significant-word heuristic only when no curated pair exists — Alex's own answer, "1 and 2 I think have their place, do both." `logic._findSubstitute` is the one real shared function both the classifier and every render site (recipe card/schedule preview/shopping list) call — a real substitute's name is shown wherever an orange row renders, never invented. `_findMakeableRecipes` now counts orange without ever blocking makeable (same "no shopping trip needed" status as green/purple). **Brown**: Oracle now tags each ingredient `is_separable:true/false` at generation time (Alex's own confirmed answer, "Oracle tags it at generation time") via a new shared `SEPARABLE_TAG_TEXT` instruction spliced into all 3 real full-recipe-generating prompts (rule 8) and a new `recipe_ingredients.is_separable` column (additive migration, `DEFAULT false` — old recipes stay honestly essential, never guessed separable, same precedent as H14's per-step measurements); `logic._sanitizeIngredientSeparable` defensively coerces the field to a real boolean on both the RECIPE_JSON and RECIPE_UPDATE_JSON parse paths (rule 5). Brown only ever classifies when a real per-session No-Shop mode is active AND the ingredient is separable — checked before orange/blue — rendering a bare "can be omitted" note, **explicitly never a computed serving-pairing suggestion**: Alex directly overrode that idea, "No, just say 'can be omitted.'" **Toggle**: new `planned_cooks.shop_mode`/`shopping_lists.shop_mode` columns (additive, `DEFAULT 'can_shop'`), a real select control in the Session Builder popup ("🛒 Can go shop" / "🚫 No shop this time"), persisted via the existing `_persistSessionDraft` insert-or-update path (rule 8) — Alex's own confirmed scope, "Per planned-cook session," not a global setting. `logic._computeIngredientUsage` gained an optional `opts` param (`{noShop}`) so brown can actually reach the shared classifier, with every existing caller that omits it completely unaffected; a merged usage group across 2+ recipes in one session stays essential if even ONE contributing recipe needs it, never silently downgraded. `logic._generateShoppingList` excludes brown-status items from the real physical shopping list entirely when No-Shop mode is active (the whole point is skipping the shop trip) and persists the list's own `shop_mode` so reopening it later shows an honest note explaining the omission. Verified via a real headless-Chromium pass, 26/26 new assertions pass, plus full re-runs of H18's 27/27, H19's 20/20, and H20's 16/16 suites confirming zero regression. `node --check` clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped `20260917b`→`20260917c`. **Untested by Alex's own hand.**
- **H20 shipped Sep 17 2026 — 3 real live schedule/planned-cook bugs fixed, real Alex report: "pressing start planned cook on schedule did nothing. scheduling planned cook did go on selected time slot - but end time was 26hrs which didnt make sense. no way to revert planned cook back from scheduled to planned cook in cooking module."** All 3 investigated fresh via real evidence — a direct Supabase query of Alex's own real `planned_cooks`/`rpgace_agendas` rows, never guessed. **Bug 1** — `startScheduledTask` (the Daily Schedule grid's "Start" button) only ever updated the local cache: zero toast, zero re-render, zero Supabase write, so a real click was indistinguishable from a broken button. Fixed with a real toast, a real `renderDailyGrid()` re-render, and a real write-through to `rpgace_agendas.started_at` (same write-proxy `scheduleToCalendar` already uses, rule 8); `completeScheduledTask` had the identical gap, fixed the same way for consistency though Alex hadn't reported it. **Bug 2** — real evidence showed the actual schedule MATH was correct (`schedule.totalMinutes: 183`, a genuine 3h3m session starting at 23:30) — the bug was a real DISPLAY bug: `_fracClock` took a raw fractional-hour value with no wrap, so 23.5+3.05=26.55 rendered as the nonsensical "26:33" instead of the real next-day "02:33." Fixed with a real modulo-24 wrap (every in-range caller unaffected) plus an honest "(+1 day)" suffix on the one real caller that can produce an out-of-range value, so a genuine cross-midnight block is shown plainly rather than implied to end before it starts. Honestly NOT built: the overflow portion doesn't also appear on tomorrow's own daily grid — a real, separate, deferred cross-day-event-splitting gap. **Bug 3** — once Accept flips a `planned_cooks` row to `status='scheduled'`, it became completely invisible to the Cooking module (`_loadDraftSession` only ever checks `status='draft'`), with zero path back from the Agenda entry into Cooking's own UI. Built new `logic._loadScheduledSession`/`ui._showScheduledCookInfo`/`logic._revertScheduleToDraft` — the "🍳 Planned Cook" hub button now shows a real "Scheduled for &lt;date&gt; — view / revert" state instead of silently hiding it, with a deliberate (never automatic) "↩ Revert to draft" action that flips the row back, best-effort cleans up the stale `rpgace_agendas` row AND the local `RPGACE.DB('sched')` cache, and rehydrates `_session` so the Session Builder reopens with the same real recipes. Verified via a real headless-Chromium pass, 16/16 new assertions pass, plus full re-runs of H18's 27/27 and H19's 20/20 suites confirming zero regression. `node --check` clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped `20260917a`→`20260917b`. Full record: `records/2026-09/h20_schedule_start_revert_midnight_bugs_2026-09-17.txt`. **Untested by Alex's own hand.**
- **H19 shipped Sep 17 2026 — recipes now lean on cups (liquids/spices) + weight when it makes sense, and the 5-color system gained real cross-unit bridging so it still works once stock stays logged in weight/ml/count, real Alex ask (stated twice): "also use cups as much as possible (liquid and spices) and weight when makes sense, everywhere where a recipe is present. in pantry ill be using mostly weight and ml and count rather than cups."** Real engineering consequence identified before building, not guessed after a bug report (rule 1): H18's exact-unit-match system would have silently turned every liquid/spice ingredient red the moment recipes started using cups while stock stayed in g/ml/count — a foreseeable regression to a feature shipped the day before, fixed pre-emptively. **Only 2 real, never-guessed bridges added, per the project's own standing "no invented conversion" rule**: (1) `logic.VOLUME_TO_ML` (cup=240ml, tbsp=15ml, tsp=5ml, l=1000ml) — real, exact, ingredient-independent physics, a cup is always the same real volume regardless of contents; (2) a weight bridge using the RECIPE'S OWN already-existing `grams_estimate` (built for nutrition, H2 — reused, rule 8, never a new invented per-ingredient density table) when a recipe's unit isn't weight but a real stock bucket is. Genuinely NOT bridged, on purpose: "count" against weight/volume (a real onion's weight varies — no honest conversion exists, stays an honest non-match, rule 7). New shared `logic._resolveHaveAmount`/`_convertVolumeUnits` (rule 8) replace H18's flat exact-key `byKey` lookup with `logic._loadPantrySummed`'s new `byIngredient` (array of every real unit-bucket per ingredient, needed for the bridge to have something to bridge FROM) — used by all 3 real consumers (`_computeIngredientUsage`, `_findMakeableRecipes`, `ui._showRecipeCard`'s renderIngredients). New shared `UNIT_PREFERENCE_TEXT` ("prefer cup measurements for liquids and spices... prefer weight in grams for other ingredients... when that makes more practical sense — never cups just for the sake of it") spliced into all 4 real generation-adjacent prompts (full generation, H15 chat-triggered generation, the RECIPE_UPDATE_JSON critique-revision context, Suggest Additions). Verified via a real headless-Chromium pass, 20/20 new assertions pass (exact volume conversions both directions with a real null-guard on non-volume units; the weight bridge correctly derives a real grams-per-cup ratio from a specific recipe's own `grams_estimate`; a genuinely unbridgeable case honestly returns 0; full `_computeIngredientUsage`/`_findMakeableRecipes`/`_showRecipeCard` integration; the real outbound prompt carries the new instruction), plus a full re-run of H18's own 27/27 suite confirming zero regression from the `byKey`→`byIngredient` shape change. `node --check` clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped `20260916j`→`20260917a`. Full record: `records/2026-09/h19_cup_weight_unit_bridging_2026-09-17.txt`. **Untested by Alex's own hand.**
- **H18 shipped Sep 16 2026 — a real 5-color ingredient-status system (🟢 plenty/🟡 not enough/🔴 don't have/🔵 have an alternative, buy the real one/🟣 will run low, restock after this cook), applied everywhere ingredients are shown, plus a new "🥫 What Can I Cook Right Now?" saved-recipe finder and a stock-only generation constraint — real Alex ask, verbatim: "green ingredients are what i have, yellow are not what enough of, red is what i dont have, blue is what i have as an alternative, but should buy in shop... purple is what will almost run out and should be replenished next shop after planned cooking session... now i can look for recipes that will only give me purple or green ingredients."** Real `/interrogation` (4 questions, full record `records/2026-09/h18_ingredient_color_system_spec_2026-09-16.txt`): blue = a same-aisle-group alternative present in stock (H13's `_classifyAisle`, reused); purple threshold = have-minus-needed margin below 20% of needed; scope = **everywhere** ingredients are shown, replacing H14b's older green/gold 2-color scheme rather than sitting alongside it; the "look for recipes" ask combines a browse-filter over saved recipes AND a real generation-time constraint. One shared `logic._classifyIngredientStatus`/`INGREDIENT_STATUS_META`/`ui._renderIngredientStatusRow` (rule 8) now drives the recipe card, the schedule preview (real 3-bucket regroup: buy-before / plenty / restock-after, matching Alex's own stated timing logic), and the shopping list. **Real mid-build correction #1**: Alex caught "not pantry only, stock only, pantry is part of stock, like fridge and freezer" while the feature was still named `pantryOnly` — renamed throughout (`stockOnly`, `_generateWithStockConstraint`); the underlying data was always all 3 `pantry_stock` locations (H11), only the naming was wrong. **Real mid-build correction #2, a genuine live bug caught from Alex's own use of the Schedule Cook preview**: "ingredients that are not equal to measurements in recipes show as not in stock." Root cause — every have-vs-needed match keyed strictly on an EXACT unit string, so "gram" (recipe) vs "g" (stock), or "cloves" vs "clove", silently failed to match, showing red for something genuinely in stock. Fixed with one shared `logic._normalizeUnit` (a curated synonym table + a safe generic plural strip, never a cross-system numeric conversion — g never becomes oz), used everywhere a (ingredient_id, unit) key is built for this system. New `logic._findMakeableRecipes`/`ui._showStockMatchFinder`: every saved recipe classified live, a recipe is "makeable" only when every ingredient is green or purple; clicking one reopens it via the new `logic._loadSavedRecipe` + `ui._showRecipeCard`'s new optional `existingRecipeId` param (prevents a real duplicate-save bug on a reopened recipe). `logic._generate` gained an optional `stockOnly` param — a real CHOICE constraint (use only what's in stock), never a fabricated quantity guarantee; a genuinely empty stock list fails loud rather than silently sending the unconstrained prompt. Verified via a real headless-Chromium pass, 27/27 assertions pass (unit normalization incl. the "oz" false-strip guard and the real reported gram/g bug reproduced-then-fixed; all 5 status outcomes plus the 20%-boundary edge case; recipe-card/finder rendering and click-through; the stock-only prompt content and its real empty-stock fail-loud path). `node --check` clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped `20260916i`→`20260916j`. **Untested by Alex's own hand.**
- **H17 shipped Sep 16 2026 — recipe generation (and its "Suggest 5 ideas" narrow-down) no longer hangs silently forever on a slow/failed Oracle reply, real Alex report ("5 recipes not generating, with narrow down drop list... do it for when I'm ever generating recipes").** Real root cause, found by reading the actual code (no error_log/session_memory record of the original report existed — investigated fresh, not recovered): `visualOracle._captureNextResponse` (the ONE shared Oracle-reply capture mechanism every trailer-based flow uses) has 2 real failure paths — a busy-guard early-return and its own 90s timeout — and BOTH silently released the capture lock without ever invoking the caller's callback. Every consumer showing a loading/disabled button while waiting (Generate a recipe, Suggest 5 ideas, Suggest additions) had zero way to learn the wait had failed — the button just stayed stuck on its loading text forever, indistinguishable from "nothing is happening." **Real fix, backward compatible (rule 8)**: `_captureNextResponse(callback, onFail)` gained a real, OPTIONAL 2nd param — `onFail(reason)`, `reason` one of `'busy'`|`'timeout'` — firing on both failure paths. Every EXISTING 1-arg caller (4 real non-cooking call sites: Visual Treatment/Beat Log/director-picker/Content Pipeline doc-saves) is completely unaffected, since `onFail` is simply `undefined` there — their own pre-existing "hangs silently" gap is untouched, not fixed and not worsened, deliberately out of scope (rule 11, proportionate to what Alex actually asked). The 3 real cookingOracle recipe-generation call sites opted in: `logic._generate` (clears its own `_generateFlowArmedUntil` guard on failure too, so H15's chat listener doesn't stay wrongly suppressed), `logic._suggestDishes` (the "🎲 Suggest 5 ideas" button Alex actually named), `logic._suggestIngredientAdditions` — all 3 already had fully correct `if (err) {...reset button, show error...}` handling waiting for a real error that could never arrive; this fix is purely about actually invoking that already-correct handling. Verified via a real headless-Chromium pass, 7/7 assertions pass: the busy path calls `onFail('busy')` synchronously with the success callback never firing; an existing 1-arg caller is completely unaffected (no exception, no behavior change); the real 90s timeout (forced to fire after 20ms via a scoped `setTimeout` override — the real production 90000ms value in shipped code is untouched) calls `onFail('timeout')` and releases the lock; all 3 cookingOracle call sites surface a real honest error instead of hanging on a real timeout; a genuine successful reply (fired through the real hook pipeline) still works exactly as before, zero regression. Zero console errors. `node --check` clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped `20260916h`→`20260916i`. Full record: `records/2026-09/h17_captureNextResponse_failloud_2026-09-16.txt`. **Real, honest caveat**: this was diagnosed structurally, not reproduced live — if generation still silently hangs after this ships, the real cause is elsewhere (most likely `sendToOracle` itself failing to send, which already surfaces its own separate, earlier "Could not send to Oracle" error in each of these 3 functions). **Untested by Alex's own hand.**
- **H16 shipped Sep 16 2026 — the Planned Cook session gets a real per-recipe delete button, real Alex ask ("planned cook doesnt have a delete button, i wanted to delete the lime orange desert and choose one i have all ingredients for").** `logic._addRecipeToSession` (H6) could only ever grow a session — no way existed to drop a bad pick without abandoning the whole plan. Fixed with the same real 2-click arm/confirm ✕ delete already used elsewhere in this exact module (Current Stock, H11) and across the project (rule 8) — a real `logic._removeRecipeFromSession(id, cb)` mirrors `_persistSessionDraft`'s existing insert-or-update path rather than inventing a second write mechanism: a real UPDATE with the trimmed `recipe_ids` in the common case, a real DELETE on the whole `planned_cooks` row if the removal empties the session (an empty draft serves no purpose and would otherwise resurface as a stale "0 recipes" resume — clearing `self._session` to null fixes the dashboard card's live count for free), or a pure in-memory update for the narrow never-persisted-yet edge case (`plannedCookId` still null). **Real fail-loud discipline (rule 7)**: in-memory state only mutates AFTER the real write confirms, never optimistically — a failed write surfaces as a toast with the row left un-armed, never silently diverging from what's actually saved. On success the removed row disappears from the popup in place (stays open, so the existing "+ Add another saved recipe" / "...or generate a new one" buttons are right there to pick a real replacement) unless it was the last recipe, in which case the whole popup closes rather than showing dead Schedule/Shopping-list buttons pointing at nothing. Verified via a real headless-Chromium pass, 4/4 assertions pass (real arm→confirm icon sequence + correct UPDATE write when removing 1 of 2; popup-closes + real DELETE write when removing the last recipe; a single click only arms, zero write fires; the never-persisted edge case resolves in-memory with zero writes), zero console errors. `node --check` clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped `20260916g`→`20260916h`. Full record: `records/2026-09/h16_planned_cook_delete_recipe_2026-09-16.txt`. **Untested by Alex's own hand.**
- **H15 shipped Sep 16 2026 — recipe generation can now be triggered from GENERAL Oracle chat (main dashboard chat or the floating 🔮 overlay), not only the dedicated Cooking → "⚡ Generate a recipe" button, real Alex ask ("yes, general chat too — build it") directly answering the open fork H14b's own investigation had flagged rather than guessed at.** No 4th `window.callOracle` wrapper added (the standing landmine this project already documents) — a new `recipeGenHit` gate rides `oracleAppGrounding`'s existing universal wrap, exactly matching its already-established `anatomyHit`/`actionHit` cross-module guarded-call pattern. `cookingOracle.RECIPE_GEN_KEYWORDS` is a real, curated compound-phrase list (never bare adjectives, same collision discipline H13's aisle keywords already proved), deliberately anchored on a real recipe/dish noun so "generate a quest"/"make a caption" elsewhere in the app never false-fires. A new persistent listener, `logic._listenForChatGeneratedRecipes()`, reuses the EXISTING `oracle:response-scanned` hook (already fires for both chat surfaces) rather than inventing a new event, and opens the exact same real recipe card the dedicated button flow does on a real parsed `RECIPE_JSON:` reply. **Real double-fire race solved, not ignored**: since the dedicated Generate-button flow's own one-shot capture could fire on the SAME reply as this new persistent listener, a future-timestamp guard (`_generateFlowArmedUntil`, cleared via a deferred `setTimeout(...,0)` so it survives the WHOLE synchronous hook-fire broadcast regardless of listener registration order) keeps them mutually exclusive; a 2nd guard (`_recipeCardOpen`) keeps this path and the separate RECIPE_UPDATE_JSON critique-revision path mutually exclusive too. `RECIPE_JSON_SHAPE(servingsBase)` — a real rule-8 dedup pulling the recipe-schema JSON literal (previously duplicated across `_generate`'s prompt and the RECIPE_UPDATE_JSON context-provider string) into one shared function both call, plus the new `buildRecipeGenBlock()`. **Real bugs found and fixed during verification, not shipped blind**: the initial keyword list had `'make a recipe'` but not the more natural `'make me a recipe'` (a real headless test caught this failing) — fixed in production, not just the test; and the verification test itself needed a fix (re-invoking `oracleAppGrounding.init()` mid-test is a real no-op against `window._appGroundingPatched`'s standing once-only guard, since the real page boot had already patched it — the guard itself is correct production behavior, the test needed to reset it first). Verified via a real headless-Chromium pass, 6/6 assertions pass (5 trigger-phrase cases including a deliberate false-positive guard plus the card-open suppression; the block's real content; the wrap genuinely extending the real outbound system prompt; a chat-only reply opening exactly 1 real card; the double-fire guard; the card-open guard), zero console errors. `node --check` clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped `20260916f`→`20260916g`. Full record: `records/2026-09/h15_general_chat_recipe_generation_2026-09-16.txt`. **Honest, named limitation carried over unchanged from H14b**: chat-triggered generation does NOT get the dedicated flow's short-description "Narrow it down" disambiguation step — Oracle answers directly from whatever was typed, same as any other conversational reply; whether it should also get narrow-down is a real, separate open question, not yet put to Alex. **Untested by Alex's own hand.**
- **H14 shipped Sep 16 2026 — the scheduled-cook proposed plan now shows real per-step ingredient measurements plus a real pantry-usage/grocery-need preview, real Alex ask ("show me measurements of each ingredient at each step so i dont have to go back and fourth to agree plan... a list of ingredients i will use up and what will need to be added to future grocery list"); a real same-day follow-up (H14b) added colour-coding.** No schema change — `recipes.steps` is jsonb, so a new per-step field is a pure prompt-shape change. `logic._generate`'s prompt (and the matching `RECIPE_UPDATE_JSON` critique-revision prompt, kept in sync per rule 8) now asks Oracle to tag each step with `ingredients_used:[{name,amount,unit}]`, splitting an ingredient's amount across steps if it's genuinely used more than once; `logic._sanitizeStepIngredientsUsed` defensively strips malformed entries (rule 5 — never trust model JSON blindly). One shared `ui._renderStepIngredients(step)` renders the real measurement chips, used by BOTH the single-recipe card's Method list and the schedule preview's per-block loop. **Honest, named limitation**: only recipes generated from now on carry this — old recipes' steps show no chips rather than a guessed one. **A real, live bug found and fixed while building this**: `logic._loadPantry` (backing `_generateShoppingList`) grouped `pantry_stock` rows by `ingredient_id` alone and kept whichever row arrived last — silently correct by accident until H11 (earlier the same day) added a real `location` column making 2-3 rows per ingredient normal; since H11 shipped, every real shopping-list generation has been understating "have" amounts for anything stocked in more than one place. Fixed by replacing it with `logic._loadPantrySummed` (genuinely sums across locations, matching the already-correct `_loadPantryNamed` sibling) inside a new shared `logic._computeIngredientUsage(recipeIds, cb)` (rule 8 — extracted from `_generateShoppingList`'s own prior inline logic, which now calls it instead of duplicating the math) — the fix benefits both the real shopping-list write path and the new schedule-preview panel identically. `_showSchedulePreview` now renders "📦 Will use from pantry" / "🛒 Need to add to grocery list" (aisle-sorted via H13's `_classifyAisle`, reused not reinvented) as a real, read-only preview — deliberately not a second write mechanism; the session builder's own "Generate shopping list" button is still the one real place a `shopping_lists` row gets created. **H14b, same-session follow-up from Alex's own live hand-test of the Oracle chat**: real green=have/gold=need-to-buy colour coding (matching the app's existing green/gold convention) applied to both the new usage panel and the pre-existing real `_showShoppingList` popup. A second H14b ask ("generate new recipe... didnt do narrowing down") was investigated with real evidence (every real caller of `logic._generate` grepped — narrow-down's gate is intact and the ONLY real entry point) rather than guessed at: the real, evidence-based finding is that narrow-down is scoped to one specific button (Cooking → "Generate a recipe") and never runs from free-form Oracle chat, and nothing persistently saves a bare `RECIPE_JSON:` from general chat either — a real, structural design gap, not a code defect, flagged to Alex as a genuine open fork needing his own answer before any build (rule 5), with a concrete working interim path handed to him (the dedicated Generate button, which already fully works). Verified via 2 real headless-Chromium passes: 19 assertions on the core build (sanitize, render, the pantry cross-location sum fix, `_generateShoppingList` regression, full schedule-preview render) + 4 on the colour-coding (real inline `border-left`/`color` style checked directly). `node --check` clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped `20260916d`→`20260916f`. - **H13 shipped Sep 16 2026 — the grocery list's "Need to buy" section now auto-sorts by aisle, real Alex ask ("too much scrolling and calculating needed").** Real design choice, checked before building: a new `ingredients.aisle` Supabase column was considered and rejected — a migration is always Tier-3 (needs a real stop-and-confirm outside a ratified plan or `/auto`), and a DB column would still need a real backfill for existing ingredients and a second write for every brand-new one. Built instead as a pure client-side, zero-AI-cost, zero-schema-change keyword classifier (same mechanical-tier discipline as `cofid_foods`' own `base_ingredient`/`prep_state` grouping, rule 12's guide) — `cookingOracle.logic._AISLE_GROUPS` (9 real buckets: Fruit & Veg/Meat & Fish/Bakery/Pantry & Tins/Spices,Sauces & Oils/Dairy & Chilled/Frozen/Drinks/Household & Other catch-all) + `logic._classifyAisle(name)` (lowercase substring match, first hit wins, unmatched falls to the honest catch-all rather than crashing). `ui._showShoppingList`'s "Need to buy" section (the one actually used while shopping — "Already have enough"/"Already bought" left untouched) sorts by a real walk-order then name, rendering a "📍 Aisle" subheading per group. **A real bug found and fixed during verification, not shipped blind**: a first pass wrongly classified "Sichuan Peppercorn" as Fruit & Veg (bare "pepper" matched inside "peppercorn") and "Vanilla Ice Cream" as Spices (bare "vanilla" beat Frozen's own "ice cream" phrase) — the same keyword-substring collision class already named in Known Landmines for taxonomy keywords, now proven to apply here too. Fixed with compound phrases listed ahead of the colliding bare words, plus splitting match-PRIORITY (array position) from render-WALK-ORDER (a separate `walkOrder` field) so Frozen is checked early (catches "ice cream" before Spices/Dairy's bare keywords) but still renders LAST (matching a real shop walk, bought last so it doesn't thaw). Verified via a real headless-Chromium pass (Supabase mocked): 15/15 direct classify cases pass (including both fixed collisions), full render-order assertion passes, zero real console errors. `node --check` clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped `20260916c`→`20260916d`. - **H12 shipped Sep 16 2026 — real, live bug fix: a planned cook (2 real recipes) silently vanished with no trace, no grocery list either.** Alex's own verbatim report: "i just planned 2 meals for a planned cook... but they didnt save in app, and the grocery list is nowhere to be seen." Real diagnosis via direct Supabase query (rule 4): both recipes genuinely saved (`recipes`/`recipe_ingredients`), but `planned_cooks`/`shopping_lists` were both empty and zero errors were logged anywhere — root cause was `_session` (which recipes are batched for a cook) being PURE in-memory JS state, with `planned_cooks` only ever written at the very end of the flow (Generate Shopping List or Schedule→Accept); anything before that — closing the popup, navigating away, a reload — lost the plan with zero warning. Alex's follow-up: "build so this doesnt happen, it should be shown." **Real fix**: `planned_cooks` gained a real draft lifecycle (`schedule`/`scheduled_at` made nullable, new `status` column: `'draft'`|`'scheduled'`) — a row is now created the moment the FIRST recipe joins a session (`logic._persistSessionDraft`, insert-or-update on the same row, rule 8) and kept in sync as more join, so nothing is ever silently lost again. `logic._loadDraftSession` resumes a real draft (with real recipe titles joined in) on the next Cooking-hub open, same page load or a fresh one — `openMain()` and `ui._showModuleHub()` both check it before falling through to "no session." `_acceptSchedule` now UPDATEs the same draft row in place (`status:'scheduled'`) instead of always inserting a fresh one. The session-builder popup's own title ("Recipe saved" — true only of the individual recipe, part of the original confusion) is now "Planned Cook" with a real "✅ Saved — safe to close this and come back later" line. Verified via 5 real headless-Chromium assertions (a real in-memory fake `planned_cooks` table backing the mock, not just call-shape checks): first-add insert, second-add update-not-duplicate, fresh-load resume with real titles joined, the hub showing the real resumed count, and Accept updating the same row in place (including the real unmocked `scheduleToCalendar()` legacy call writing a real `rpgace_agendas` row and back-link). `node --check` clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped `20260916b`→`20260916c`. ## Oracle section addendum — collapsed from CLAUDE.md Sep 23 2026 (DD-1 batch 2, real Oversight Doc Dedup & Compression pass)
Full narrative moved here, verbatim, per the July 31 collapse rule -- the durable rule statement itself stays in CLAUDE.md, only the dated recurrence narrative moves.

- **`oracleAppGrounding.SELF_KNOWLEDGE` must be updated in the same session as this Current State section** — it is a hand-maintained digest Oracle answers from, and a stale one makes Oracle confidently wrong about its own app. **Fixed July 31** (this bullet's own "stale right now" warning had itself gone stale — the live string at `rpgace_core.js:2870` already says "Exactly 12 of 21… (numbers 1-10, 11, 14)," correctly reflecting the July 30 phylum-11/14 build; only this warning hadn't been updated to match). Real lesson worth keeping: a warning about staleness is itself a claim that can go stale — re-verify it against the live code before trusting it, same rule 1 as everywhere else. **The exact predicted failure mode recurred, much worse, Sep 23 2026 — real `/paranoia` pass, triggered by Alex's own philosophical question ("is oversight docs just an appendix… or a proper representation").** Direct read found SELF_KNOWLEDGE's own last real date-stamped fact was **Aug 26 2026 — 28 days stale**, and it still asserted an explicitly FALSE claim ("Quest Board has zero persistence") that `questEngine` had made untrue back on Aug 23-24 — meaning a real user asking Oracle whether quest completions actually save would have gotten a confidently wrong answer for a month. Commit-archaeology found why: 66 of the last 124 commits touched CLAUDE.md (constant attention, cheap to edit) vs. only 15 touching `SELF_KNOWLEDGE:` at all, none adding real Sep 15-22 content — the artifact competing with real code changes, with nothing watching it the way `node --check` watches syntax, loses. **Real fix**: rewritten from scratch, 66,332→11,205 chars (83% smaller) while covering everything real through Sep 22 — the false Quest Board claim corrected, the old bloated Galaxy Map G1-G76 narrative pruned to current-state-only facts (same prune discipline this file itself got July 31; the narrative still lives in patch_notes.html/CLAUDE_archive.md, not lost). **The real, honest verdict from that pass, not just the fix**: oversight docs are NOT, by default, a proper standing representation of the total system — the process/rules layer is genuinely load-bearing (this file governs real session behavior), but the facts-of-state layer (module counts, feature status, Oracle's own self-knowledge) is structurally an appendix, accurate only immediately after someone manually re-checks it, with no mechanism that notices drift in between. Alex chose "fix the content now" over "build the enforcement mechanism first" (a real, explicit fork, not guessed) — **the mechanism gap stays open, named, not solved by this pass**: nothing yet catches SELF_KNOWLEDGE going stale again the way the Galaxy Map session-start check catches module/page-count drift. Full record: `records/2026-09/oversight_docs_appendix_or_representation_paranoia_2026-09-23.txt`.

## Oracle domain narrative — collapsed from CLAUDE.md Sep 23 2026 (DD-1 batch 2, real Oversight Doc Dedup & Compression pass)
Full dated stories, verbatim, moved here per the July 31 collapse rule. Each now has a real anchor link from CLAUDE.md's own durable one-liner into the matching patch_notes.html card.

- **`visualOracle._captureNextResponse` is a bare one-shot listener on the shared `oracle:response-scanned` hook with no request correlation, and `RPGACE.hooks.fire` broadcasts to every registered listener — arming this capture before confirming the matching `sendToOracle()` call actually sent (not silently blocked by the in-flight guard) leaves it dangling, ready to wrongly consume a different reply.** Fixed Aug 6: `sendToOracle()`/the wrapped `sendChat()` now report whether a send actually went through; every real call site (Visual Oracle's command panel, Beat Log's F18 auto-chain, `_sendFilledPromptToOracle` — the shared tail Visual Treatment AND Captions both use) sends first and only arms if confirmed sent. Beat Log's two Oracle auto-fires (the combined "Log Beat + Find Artists" prompt, F18's Visual Treatment chain) became real human-gated buttons per Alex's own direct hand-test report, which also removes the unsupervised async window this bug lived in. `engineer_pass_2026-08-06_10.txt`. **Untested — needs Alex to run the real flow again.**
- **Oracle architecture/anatomy grounding is now LIVE (July 31, Alex-confirmed build).** A new `oracle_module_anatomy` table (13 rows — riskiest/most complex modules, Alex's own scope choice: `dashDeck`, `phylumPath`, `authGate`, `oracleAppGrounding`, `oracleTreeGrounding`, `bookworm`, `config`, `taxonomyTree`, `leftNav`, `beatLog`, `contentProductionLive`, `videoPipeline`, `voiceInput`), `anon_read_only`+`authenticated_all` RLS, deliberately excluded from `api/data-write.js`. `oracleAppGrounding` gained a second keyword gate (`ANATOMY_KEYWORDS`) riding inside its existing `window.callOracle` wrap (not a 4th wrapper) plus `_buildAnatomyBlock()`, reusing `oracleTreeGrounding`'s scan→SELECT→score→top-N retrieval shape. Oracle-only by design — Claude Code sessions already have better direct access via the repo + `graphify-out/`; building a shared copy would have been a real rule-8 violation, confirmed by an independent Opus Council of 5 review. **Hand-tested and confirmed working by Alex same session**: both a `dashDeck` and an `authGate` question returned real, correctly-cited grounding (source_ref, gotchas, connects_to) with no invented content. Full record: `oracle_architecture_anatomy_db_debate_2026-07-31.txt`.
- **A real Mock Oracle testing mode exists (Aug 6, built while Alex was out of real Anthropic credits).** A single check inside `callOracle()` itself in main.js (not an outer wrap — deliberately avoids racing the 3 existing grounding wraps' own init order) intercepts before the real `/api/oracle` fetch when `mockOracle.isEnabled()`, returning a synthetic, unmistakably-labeled reply (`🧪 [MOCK ORACLE...]`) instead. Every real button/DOM/hook/Supabase-write path downstream still runs authentically on top of the fake text — this exercises real app wiring end-to-end with zero API cost, it does not fake output quality. Off by default; toggled via a real fixed red/green slide switch pinned to the top of every page (`🧪 Dummy Oracle` red ↔ `✅ Oracle API` green), always visible regardless of state — **redesigned Aug 6 from the original bottom-right circular button after Alex's real hand-test report ("i cant see dummy oracle right now... it is not there")**: the old design only showed a warning banner while active and competed for space with `voiceInput`'s own floating mic button, a real discoverability bug, not a logic bug (the service worker was checked and ruled out — `sw.js` is deliberately network-first for JS/HTML, confirmed not a caching issue). New `mockOracle` module, `rpgace_core.js`. **Untested by hand.** **Aug 6, same session — Alex's own workflow confirmation**: dummy mode is for "developing functions and DOMs" (build/wiring, zero API cost), the toggle back to real Oracle is for "when I need to check how output is formed" (real quality checks) — exactly the split the module was built for. The underlying pattern is now also a real **global** Claude Code skill, `~/.claude/skills/dummy/SKILL.md` — generalizes RPGACE's own `mockOracle` (the reference implementation) into a portable "default to a project's own dummy AI mode while building wiring, real API only for an explicit output-quality pass" discipline, usable in any future project the same way the global `omnitrix`/`aintergration` skills already are. Pairs with `/Engineer`: build+initial-verify stages default to dummy mode, one targeted real-API pass checks actual output quality before a pass reports done.
- **The Mock Oracle toggle is now a real 3-mode Oracle Mode switch (Aug 6, same day 2nd pass): Real / Dummy / Fallback Scout, cycling on click of the same top-right switch.** Alex's own spec: "in the toggle in top right as a third mode, think of those oracle functions as modes." Fallback Scout mode queues every real Oracle send into `oracle_fallback_queue` (`context.type:'scout_item'`, same `RPGACE.sb.secureWrite` path `phylumPath._queueFallback` already uses) instead of calling the live API, returning an honest, unmistakably-labeled acknowledgment with the real live queue depth — never a fake answer to the actual question. Answered for free by the existing daily "RPGACE Fallback Drain" Routine, unchanged. A new "📥 Scouted, Now Answered" popup list (`dashDeck._popup()`) browses every scout_item row, pending and answered — this is the real named view Alex asked for ("1. scouted, now answered"). Fail-loud fix in the same pass: a failed queue write now says so explicitly in the reply instead of silently claiming success (rule 7). **Confirmed, honest limitation, not built blind**: the deployed web app cannot auto-fire the Fallback Drain early at "10 items waiting" — `create_trigger`'s real minimum interval is hourly, and firing a trigger needs an authenticated Claude Code Remote session, which the public anon-key app is not; the queue-depth message flags this plainly at 10+ pending items rather than faking an auto-fire. Full verbatim record, including the real Anthropic API pricing answer Alex asked for and the `/investor` persona's pros/cons on the API-key question: `oracle_3mode_toggle_and_investor_skill_spec_backlog_2026-08-06.txt`. **Untested by hand.**
- **Real `/deduplication` fix, same day 3rd pass: Fallback Scout's repeated-entry problem had a real root cause, not a queue-layer bug.** Alex noticed the same encyclopedia entries (Harp Arpeggiation, Wiley's Eskimo, etc.) appearing multiple times in "📥 Scouted, Now Answered" and asked for real dedup against RPGACE's own established content, not just token-saving in the abstract. Root cause, confirmed by direct code read: `ENC_BULLET_CACHE` (main.js, the encyclopedia preview-bullet generator) is a plain in-memory JS object with zero persistence — it evaporates on every page reload, so `generateEncBullets()` re-sent the identical prompt to Oracle (and, once Fallback mode existed, re-queued it) every time the Encyclopedia page loaded, even for entries whose bullets were already generated in a prior session. Real fix, per rule 8: new `encyclopedia.preview_bullets` jsonb column (migration applied) — the entry's own real "destination of planned output" — checked BEFORE ever calling Oracle; a successful generation persists there via `RPGACE.sb.secureWrite` (encyclopedia is already in `api/data-write.js`'s `ALLOWED_TABLES`), a transient failure never does, so it can't poison the row with the placeholder fallback bullet. Separate, caller-agnostic safeguard added at the queue layer itself: `mockOracle._queueScoutItem` now checks existing pending `scout_item` rows for an exact prompt-text match before inserting, and returns an honest "already scouted" acknowledgment instead of minting a duplicate — protects every current and future Fallback Scout caller, not just this one root cause. `node --check` clean on both files. **Untested by hand.**

Honest limitation stated in the skill itself: a checklist doesn't enforce itself — this makes the existing rule 6 obligation checkable, it doesn't guarantee it's followed. Separately, a real Dummy-Oracle-mode critique ("no context on what's injected") was `/paranoia`'d and planned as a future trace-log feature (structured codes for hook fires + Supabase writes during a dummy run) — NOT built this pass, per Alex's own instruction; 3 real open forks (trace-code format, display surface, persistence) need his answers before it's picked up. Full verbatim record, both ideas: `dummy_mode_tracelog_and_update_logging_system_paranoia_2026-08-06.txt`.
- **Live graphify demo + real `/paranoia`+`/restructure` verdict on plugging it into `/update-logging-system` (Aug 6, same day 5th pass).** Real evidence, not the earlier same-day quick miss: that earlier "graphify is stale" query had skipped `references/query.md`'s own mandatory vocab-expansion step — done properly (`graphify query "self knowledge oracle grounding" --budget 1200`), it returned a genuinely good, correctly-cited cross-doc answer. **Separately confirmed, real and structural, not staleness**: `graphify explain "mockOracle"`/`"phylumPath"` still return "No node matching" even after a live `graphify . --update --code-only --no-viz` rebuild — `RPGACE.register('name', {...})`, this project's own standing module convention, is never extracted as a first-class symbol by graphify's AST extractor (only generic function declarations are); this is a real, structural gap a rebuild does not fix, not to be re-attempted as "just needs a refresh." Verdict: **Adopt narrowly** — `/update-logging-system` gains artifact type 10 (run the free `--code-only` rebuild whenever `rpgace_core.js`/`main.js`/`api/*.js` structure changes, keeping the index from drifting further even though it can't close the extraction gap) and an optional, explicitly non-authoritative doc-cross-reference pre-check. **Explicitly NOT adopted**: plugging graphify into `Routine`/`scope`/`Summary`/`GODMODE`/`commit-archaeologist`/`Engineer` as a blanket evidence-gathering replacement — real per-skill reasons in the record, chief one being that overselling a tool that's confirmed unable to answer RPGACE's own module-structure questions would create false confidence, which costs more than admitted ignorance. Full verbatim record, live demo transcript, and the real rundown of how to use/read graphify: `graphify_restructure_paranoia_2026-08-06.txt`. **Same day, 6th pass, 2 real follow-ups Alex asked for directly**: (1) `/5thDimension`'s own Phase 1 now runs the same free `--code-only` rebuild as part of establishing "what's actually built" — referenced from `update-logging-system`'s artifact type 10, not duplicated (rule 8). (2) A real recolor pass: `graphify export html`'s default Tableau10 palette was swapped to RPGACE's own real `style.css` tokens (`--gold`/`--blue`/`--green`/`--purple`/`--red`/`--orange`/`--gold2`/`--hp-col`/`--mp-col`/`--muted` — all 10 sourced, none guessed) via a new standalone script, `scripts/graphify_recolor.py` — deliberately NOT a patch to graphify's own installed package (external, upgradeable tooling; a hand-edit there would be lost on the next upgrade and isn't RPGACE's own code to maintain). Idempotent by design (always maps FROM the fixed Tableau10 defaults, so re-running after every fresh `graphify export html` is always correct). Wired into `update-logging-system`'s artifact type 10 as a standing step, not a one-off. **Same day, 7th pass — `graph.html` promoted to a real, accessible oversight surface, per Alex's direct ask.** Real evidence check first: no `.vercelignore` exists, and `patch_notes.html`/`manual.html`/`taxonomy_map.html`/`minotaur_map.html` all already serve live from `rpgace.vercel.app` with zero explicit `vercel.json` rewrite — confirms Vercel's filesystem-first static serving already covers `graphify-out/graph.html` without needing a new rewrite entry (checked, not assumed). Added as a new "Auto-generated" group in the real, live oversight popup (`dashDeck._openOversight` — the ONE live list; `docsLinks._inject` was independently found already neutered/dead since July 20, correctly self-documented as an audit trail, not a second live copy needing a rule-8 fix). `update-logging-system`'s artifact type 10 upgraded from "regenerate the HTML if wanted" to mandatory: every code-structure change now also re-runs `graphify export html` + the recolor script + commits the refreshed file, since Alex can now click through to it from inside the live app and a stale link there would be worse than no link.
- **Real `/paranoia`+`/interrogation` on connecting graphify/interconnection_map.md/taxonomy_placement_rules.txt/skills to minotaur_map.html, plus river-grouping graphify (Aug 6, 8th pass).** Real evidence found minotaur_map.html is deliberately a RARE-TOUCH, hand-authored doc (CLAUDE.md's own rule: update only on a new wing) whose existing relationship to `system_flow_map.md` is already a manual "direct plain-language translation," not an automated pipeline — a live auto-generation build would work against that stated design goal. 3 real forks put to Alex directly rather than guessed, all answered with the recommended option: (1) consistency-CHECK, not live auto-generation; (2) skills cross-referenced for consistency only, never folded into river content (they document Claude Code's own dev process, not app information flow); (3) graphify river-grouping covers named modules only, not every node. **Built**: `/update-logging-system` artifact type 11 (interconnection_map.md/taxonomy_placement_rules.txt/skill changes affecting an existing river get flagged for a real hand-update, never silently auto-rewritten). New `scripts/graphify_river_group.py` — parses `rpgace_core.js`'s own 54 real `/* ===MODULE:x=== */` marker pairs for real line ranges, maps each to one of 11 rivers via a curated table sourced from `interconnection_map.md`'s section headers + minotaur_map.html's own river titles, recolors matched nodes with a river-specific palette and a new community id (1000+river) so the existing legend-toggle code works for rivers with zero new JS. Real, honest result: 16 of 1064 nodes matched across 7 of 11 rivers — most nodes have no single-module home and correctly stay in graphify's native coloring, the confirmed scope limit, not a bug. Wired into artifact type 10's mandatory sequence as a third step: export → recolor → river-group. Full verbatim record: `minotaur_graphify_river_paranoia_2026-08-06.txt`.
- **`oracleDevBridge`'s flag-storage truncation cap was stale, fixed Aug 6.** Real bug found during a full `oracle_dev_suggestions` review (`oracle_dev_suggestions_review_2026-08-06.txt`): the July 28 fix raised the cap from 4000→8000 chars, tuned to the THEN-current `max_tokens:1200` reply ceiling — never cross-checked when that ceiling was separately raised to 3000 on July 31. Real evidence, not guessed: 6 of 8 real flagged rows between Aug 5-6 hit the 8000-char cap almost exactly and silently lost their endings (director trailers, final scene breakdowns). Raised to 16000. Scope confirmed narrow: this only affected archival copies inside `oracle_dev_suggestions` itself — the real operational save path (`_captureNextResponse` → `creative_docs`) was never touched by this cap.
- **120 orphaned scratch scripts deleted from `scripts/` in total, real 2-round cleanup (Aug 6, 12th-13th passes, on graphify's own disconnected-node list).** Round 1 (115 files): computed real degree from `graphify-out/graph.json`'s edges — every 0-degree node was a whole-file node under `scripts/`, all one-off `open('rpgace_core.js'/'main.js')` + a single hardcoded find/replace or line-dump, zero references anywhere else in the repo, all tracing to 2 historical bulk commits (June 30 + July 7). Deleted per Alex's direct call (delete outright, git history preserves them). **Round 2 (5 more, same session, real self-check on the "10 kept" list)**: 3 more of the exact same species (`build_full_scheduling_system.py`/`rebuild_daily_grid_v2.py`/`stub_addSchedButtons.py`) had slipped past round 1's strict 0-degree filter because each called one internal helper function defined in the same file, giving them degree 1 — real evidence, not assumed. `supabase_sync.py` deleted too: its own docstring claimed "Imported by rpgace_intel.py," a real, checkable claim that turned out **false** — nothing in the repo imports it. `mod_taxonomyTree.js` deleted as a stale historical staging file (real evidence: `rpgace_build.py` — a genuinely live, well-connected "Master Build Tool," confirmed real and left untouched — has a documented `update taxonomyTree mod_taxonomyTree.js` command in patch_notes.html; the taxonomyTree module it staged is already merged into `rpgace_core.js`). `scripts/` now holds exactly 5 real files. **Real, honest finding after both rounds**: 0-degree orphans are now fully gone (950 nodes), but the graph is still 56 separate connected components (biggest 239 nodes, many small clusters) — real evidence that graphify's AST extractor cannot see `RPGACE.register()` module boundaries or `RPGACE.hooks`/`RPGACE.modules.X()` dynamic dispatch as edges (same confirmed structural gap named earlier this session), plus docs/code sitting in separate extraction domains. Not a bug to force-fix by fabricating edges — a real synthetic-edge-layer fix (grounded in real hook-name/module-name string matching, not invented) was put to Alex directly. **Decided, not left open**: "56 real components is an honest picture, leave it" — not built, and a future session should not re-propose it without a new explicit reason. **Real follow-up (Aug 6, same session, 3rd round)**: Alex asked for a real justification for keeping the 56, plus real spatial positioning of disconnected components closest to what they're actually about. Justification: an edge currently means one specific, checkable thing (a real AST-extracted reference) — mixing in weaker "probably related" edges would make every edge less trustworthy, and the disconnection itself is a useful signal of exactly where graphify's extraction has no purchase. Positioning is different from an edge (proximity ≠ a claimed hard reference), so it's a real, honest way to serve the underlying ask: `graphify_river_group.py` extended with `build_component_zone_map()` — computes every real connected component, then places it near a zone via real evidence (an existing module-marker river tag wins outright; otherwise real file-path rules: `.claude/skills/`+`plans/`+root oversight docs+dev/build tooling → new Zone XIII; `api/*.js` → new Zone XII, real since every api file routes through `api/_context.js`'s shared auth, confirmed by grep; `fourth_rota.py`/`n8n/*` → River V, real documented Schedule tooling). Result: 711 of 950 nodes now positioned (up from 16), 239 honestly left unclassified — all of them main.js's own single component, since main.js has no per-line module-marker evidence the way rpgace_core.js does. Full record: `graphify_orphan_scripts_cleanup_2026-08-06.txt`. **Real continuation (Aug 11, `/paranoia` follow-up): both of that 239-node gap's real causes closed, using the same evidence discipline, no new kind of reasoning.** (1) The old single Zone XIII (Dev Process) was one real category doing the work of four — split into 4 real sub-zones on the exact same file-path evidence already governing it, just finer-grained: Zone XIII Skills (`.claude/skills/`), Zone XIV Oversight Docs (the live-maintained doc set), Zone XV Session Records/Backlog (dated `.txt`/`.md` at repo root), Zone XVI Dev Tooling (`scripts/`, `plans/`, build config). (2) main.js's 239-node gap was a real, different mechanism problem, not more missing file-path rules: main.js is one connected component internally, so `build_component_zone_map()`'s component-vote would have wrongly lumped the whole file into one dominant neighbor's river. Fixed with a new `classify_mainjs_by_keyword()` — real per-FUNCTION classification from main.js's own 240 real names (cross-checked against actual function bodies wherever a name alone was ambiguous, e.g. confirming `renderDB`/`loadNote`/`deleteNote`/`extractTopic` are Learning-page video-notes functions, not Content Intelligence, by reading the code around them), with a real bypass in `build_component_zone_map()` so unmatched main.js nodes never inherit a tagged neighbor's river. 228 of 240 main.js nodes matched (tooltips honestly flagged `(main.js · keyword-matched)` — a real but weaker evidence tier than rpgace_core.js's structural module markers, said plainly rather than presented the same way); the remaining 12 (`main.js`/`CONFIG`/`STATE`/`LEVEL_TITLES`, the dead `updateDBStats()` stub, the cross-cutting global-text-select cluster, `initApp()`, and the new shared `_sbGet()` helper used by many domains at once) stay honestly unclassified. Also fixed in the same pass: Zone XII/XIII's old `#2ABFB0`/`#7a7a8a` swatch check — `#2ABFB0` is real (confirmed sourced from minotaur_map.html/manual.html/patch_notes.html's own shared oversight-doc `:root` palette, not style.css, but an equally real already-used token — not fabricated); `#7a7a8a` matched neither real palette and was genuinely made up, replaced with real tokens across all 4 new zones (`--amber`/`--text`/`--panel3`/`--border`). - **"Delete doesn't stick" (videoSummary._delete / clearEncyclopedia) fixed Aug 27 — real `/CEO`+`/interrogation`+`/drift`+`/paranoia` pass, `/Routine` item #1.** Root cause: `local_server.py` (Alex's own machine, outside this repo) holds its own copy of intel-report data with no concept of deletion — `syncIntelData`'s 30s recurring poll and the Encyclopedia's own "⚡ Sync & Push" button both silently merged stale, deleted content back in. Real fix, NOT a permanent per-URL ban (Alex's own correction — a bad extraction shouldn't block a future good one of the same URL): a new `intel_reanalysis_pool` table + `encyclopedia.source_url` column back one shared suppression helper (`RPGACE.utils.filterReanalysisSuppressed`) — a merge candidate is dropped only if it's the same age or older than a real deletion; anything newer passes through as a genuine fresh reanalysis. Encyclopedia gained a real "🔁 Re-analysis Pool" list Alex can browse/revisit. `refreshEncyclopediaDisplay`/`refreshJournalDisplay` also had a separate, real ambiguous-fetch bug fixed (couldn't tell "fetch failed" from "fetch succeeded with 0 rows," both fell back to stale cache). Full record: `records/2026-08/delete_doesnt_stick_tombstone_ceo_spec_2026-08-27.txt`. Flagged for Alex's real hand-test via `smoke_test_items`' `needs_confirm_highlight`. **The client-side-only residual risk this bullet originally named is now CLOSED, same day, same session**: `local_server.py`+`rpgace_intel.py` were brought into the repo (`local_server/`, Alex's own confirmed ask — real source, not a summary) and `/push-to-supabase`'s handler read directly — confirming the risk was real, not theoretical (zero dedup, zero deletion-awareness, blindly re-POSTing every local file every call). Fixed with the same 2 real conditions the client-side helper uses (existing `intel_reports` row = skip; a pending `intel_reanalysis_pool` marker at least as new as the local file's own date = skip), verified via 13 real isolated-test assertions — both `local_server.py`'s `/push-to-supabase` AND `rpgace_intel.py`'s own direct encyclopedia push also gained the same `source_url` fix (neither ever set it before, silently breaking the reanalysis-pool check for any entry pushed via either path). `local_server/README.md` has the full real account; commits `51beab3`/`b6a87fd`/`558e905`.

Real root cause, confirmed by direct code read: `mockOracle._renderState`'s full-width warning strip (`#mock-oracle-badge`, `position:fixed;top:0`) had zero compensating offset on real page content — it simply covered whatever sat at the top of `#app`. Fixed with a new `_pushBodyBelow()` — pushes `document.body`'s `padding-top` by the badge's own MEASURED `offsetHeight` (never a guessed pixel value) whenever Dummy or Fallback mode is on, clears it back to `''` the instant Real mode returns; confirmed `body` uses normal document flow (no fixed height/`overflow-y` in `style.css`), so this genuinely shifts visible content, and every other `position:fixed` overlay in the file is unaffected (fixed elements position against the viewport, not a parent's padding box). **Real rule-2/4 catch, not shipped blind**: a first version added `transition:padding-top .25s var(--ease-out)` for a real "slide" feel matching Alex's own word choice — 4 separate headless-Chromium checks (including a forced reflow + 1000ms settle wait) reproducibly showed the computed padding getting stuck at its old value after clearing it back to `''`, whether from a genuine cross-browser CSS quirk or a virtual-time-budget test-harness artifact was left unresolved rather than gambled on. ## Open-forks domain narrative — collapsed from CLAUDE.md Sep 23 2026 (DD-1 batch 4, real Oversight Doc Dedup & Compression pass)
Full dated stories, verbatim, moved here per the July 31 collapse rule.

- **A real, LIVE, unresolved production bug, found Aug 27 via a session-start error_log check, not yet fixed — quest creation is failing repeatedly.** `questEngine._createQuests` (via `RPGACE.sb.secureWrite` → `/api/data-write`) has thrown "Unexpected token '<', <!DOCTYPE" (a real HTML response where JSON was expected) across 6 distinct quest triggers, most recently within the hour before this bullet was written — this is ongoing, not historical. Real diagnostic work (Vercel MCP tools + direct code read, not guesses) RULED OUT 3 candidate causes this session: Vercel SSO/Deployment Protection blocking the whole domain (`/api/auth`/`/api/composio` succeed in the identical timeframe — ruled out); a missing `X-RPGACE-Auth` header (`requireAuth()` returns valid JSON on a real 401, never HTML — ruled out); the service worker serving a cached `index.html` as an offline fallback (`sw.js` explicitly excludes every `/api/*` path from its fetch handler — ruled out by direct read). Leading, still-unconfirmed theory: a platform-level intercept before the function is ever invoked (zero Vercel runtime-log lines for `/api/data-write` despite repeated real calls, zero clustered runtime errors) — possibly a cold-start import failure in the shared `api/data-write.js` (which also imports `archiver` for Phase H). Full trail logged in each `error_log` row's own `attempts` array. **Two concrete, cheap things only Alex can do, per rule 10**: (a) Vercel dashboard → rpgace project → current production deployment → Functions tab → open `api/data-write.js`'s own build/invocation log directly for an import-time error naming `archiver`; (b) next time the toast fires live, DevTools Network tab → the failing `/api/data-write` POST → report its actual HTTP status + first ~200 chars of the raw response body. **Separate, real, independently-confirmed finding worth its own decision, not touched this session (Tier 3 — security posture)**: Vercel's own "Vercel Authentication" (SSO) deployment-protection setting is currently ON for this project (`all_except_custom_domains`) — RPGACE already has its own password gate (`api/auth.js`+`authGate`), so this may be a redundant double-gate worth a deliberate keep-or-disable call.

- **Real, superseding correction — the bug above is CLOSED, Sep 4 2026, via `/Routine`'s own real evidence pass, not a live re-test.** Both real open questions the bullet above left for Alex got answered by a later session before he ever needed to act on them: commit `f4abb3a` (Aug 30, same day, the Fable-audit `/interrogation` round) fixed the "Unauthorized" half — `questEngine._boot` fired via `registerBootTask` before any real login, reaching `RPGACE.sb.secureWrite()` while `authGate._apiSecret` was still null (the same bug class already fixed for `morningBrief._autoRun` Aug 22 — fixed with the identical proven pattern, waiting for the real `'rpgace:login'` hook); the SAME commit answered the SSO keep-or-disable fork this bullet names — Vercel's SSO/Deployment Protection was disabled via the Vercel API, which is the real, load-bearing fix for the "Unexpected token '<', <!DOCTYPE" half (Vercel SSO intercepting an unauthenticated call and returning an HTML login page instead of JSON is exactly that symptom). Real verification, not assumed: all 12 real `error_log` rows for this bug family showed `last_seen_at` no later than Aug 28 — a genuine 5+ day clean window against the Sep 4 check date — closed with the full trail written into each row's own `rationale`/`backtrack_note`/`attempts`. Also checked directly whether any real quest content was silently lost while the bug was live (a real risk the original bullet didn't consider): `questEngine.refresh()` re-derives candidate quests fresh from live source tables on every call and dedupes against currently-open `quest_log` rows — it never consumes a one-shot queue — so nothing was lost; the same candidates will surface again on the next successful `refresh()`. **Honest caveat, stated plainly**: this is strong indirect evidence (a real code fix + a clean 5-day window + a fresh dedup-safety re-read), not a live confirmed re-test — if this exact toast fires again, it should be logged as a genuinely new `error_code`, not treated as a reopened instance of this one.

- **1 real, pre-existing dead onclick button remains: `onclick="loadDemoShifts()"` (index.html, "🔧 Load demo shifts" import button) — direct grep confirms the function doesn't exist anywhere in `rpgace_core.js`.** Clicking it throws a silent "X is not defined" visible only in DevTools console. Needs a real decision: implement the missing function, or remove the dead button — not guessed here, Alex hasn't given a directive on this one specifically. **`debugComposio()` (the other half of this landmine, found Aug 11) closed Aug 30 2026** — Alex's own Aug 23-24 smoke-test directive ("make it useful or delete it") resolved by deletion, since no real spec for a "useful" version existed. **`onclick="quickPrompt(...)"` (4 real buttons) also fails the same static-grep test, but is NOT actually dead** — `quickActions._setup()` (`rpgace_core.js:1121`) finds every real `quickPrompt` button at runtime, clones it, strips the onclick, and wires a real `addEventListener` calling `self._send(text)` instead. A deliberate runtime-patch pattern, not a bug — worth knowing so a future session doesn't conflate the two failure modes. **Separately, Alex's own Aug 23-24 smoke-test note on `quickPrompt` itself — real, superseding update, F0 shipped Sep 4 2026.** ("they actually work, but are pointless in this format — it needs to be changed by how it is wired into oracle") A real `/interrogation` pass (`records/2026-09/f0_quickprompt_oracle_rewire_spec_2026-09-04.txt`) confirmed the rewire: all 4 real quickPrompt buttons now dispatch through `oracleControl._showActionConfirm(row)` — the same real confirm+execute mechanism G41's Oracle Control already built — instead of `quickActions.ui._setup()`'s old bespoke per-button Composio-call handlers. 4 new `oracle_actions` rows (`new_quests`/`draft_email`/`yt_stats`/`log_notion`); a real, previously-unbuilt "any details to include?" optional textarea threads through to `_execute(row, details)`. **A real, load-bearing conflict was found and fixed before shipping, not silently patched around**: `contentRepurpose._restructureQuickBar()` (shipped Aug 6, predates this directive) deletes these exact 4 buttons on every boot, replacing them with a "🔀 Repurpose" button — this would have silently undone the whole rewire in production. Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` rather than guessed: stop the removal, all 5 buttons now coexist. **A second real duplicate found in passing (`_injectAgentButtons`, an Agents-page bar using the same old chat-relay pattern) — rewired Sep 22 2026, F15 (3rd of "do all 3, start with g117").** Same real rewire as F0's own `.quick-row` pattern: a fixed `ACTION_ID` map, one `oracleControl._fetchActions()` pre-fetch, each button's onclick now `oc._byId(actionId)` → `oc._showActionConfirm(row)` — the 4 bespoke closures (direct `sendToOracle`/`fillGaps`/2 raw `RPGACE.api` Composio calls) deleted entirely, using the same 4 `oracle_actions` rows F0 already created. Real pre-build check confirmed the feared conflict doesn't apply here: `_restructureQuickBar` targets only `.quick-row`/`#cr-restructured`, never `#agent-quick-btns`/`.agent-btn` — the 2 bars are genuinely independent DOM regions, no `AskUserQuestion` fork needed. Verified via 8 real headless-Chromium assertions (a real isolated harness loading unminified `rpgace_core.js`, not a stub) — a real test-construction bug was caught and fixed during the same pass, not a product bug: the module's own real `init()` boot task fires `_injectAgentButtons()` automatically the instant the script loads, before a test harness gets a chance to mock `oracleControl` — the harness now removes that real pre-existing wrap and re-invokes against the mock. All 8 pass, zero page errors. `node --check` clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped `20260922a`→`20260922b`. `ceo_plan_items` F15 marked green. **Untested by Alex's own hand.**
